“我的女儿们，”他说，“受的是淑女的教育；万一我在她们安顿之前去世，总得给她们留一份产业，使她们在受贫穷的愁容与寄人篱下的刺痛压迫时，不至落入罪恶向来向优雅多才的女子设下的那些圈套和诱惑：我的儿子们，只要收入尚可，无论置身教会、律师界还是军旅，都可以施展才能，广结朋友，在功业的基础上振兴家声。”

蒙特维尔选定从军之途时，父亲给他买了一个军职，又为他的私囊备下一份可观的钱财。“去吧，我的孩子，”他说，“去战场上寻求荣耀。你能从我这里得到的，我已尽数给你：不错，我有门路让你升迁；但请放心，除非你日后的行事配得上，这门路决不为你使。所以记住，你一生的成败全在你自己。只有一件事，我认为有责任告诫你：年轻人常常贸然缔结婚约，因自己的轻率把许多本该幸福的女子拖入贫穷愁苦的境地。一个军人，在他的军阶还没高到不必担心给世上带来一群无依无靠、生来只继承匮乏与苦难的孩子之前，无暇考虑妻室。倘若果真有一位家财足以保你处于我所教你珍视的那种独立地位的女子，慷慨地委身于一个前程主要系于战功的年轻军人——倘若这样的女子肯俯就——那么一切障碍都消除了，我将为这门预示着如许幸福的结合而欢欣。可是记住，孩子，反之，你若是轻率仓促地娶一个几乎没有家财的姑娘，把那可怜人从舒适的家和慈爱的朋友身边夺走，把她掷进微薄收入和添丁进口所能带来的全部祸患之中，那我就让你自去品尝你鲁莽的‘福果’；因为凭着一切神圣起誓，我的门路和财产都决不会为你出力。我是认真的，”他接着说，“所以把这一席话铭刻在你的记忆里，让它影响你今后的行为。你的幸福于我永远是珍贵的；我希望提醒你提防一块礁石，多少老实人的安宁都在它上面撞得粉碎；因为，相信我，最漫长的冬季战役的艰难险阻，也比不上你眼见自己选中的妻子、心爱的儿女陷于贫困愁苦、又想到正是你自己的愚蠢和莽撞铸成他们苦难的主因时，攫住你心头的锥刺。”

===Original===

As this conversation passed but a few hours before Montraville took leave of his father, it was deeply impressed on his mind: when, therefore, Belcour came with him to the place of assignation with Charlotte, he directed him to inquire of the Frenchwoman what were Miss Temple’s expectations in regard to fortune.

Mademoiselle informed him, that though Charlotte’s father possessed a genteel independence, it was by no means probable that he could give his daughter more than a thousand pounds; and in case she did not marry to his liking, it was possible he might not give her a single *sou*; nor did it appear the least likely that Mr. Temple would agree to her union with a young man on the point of embarking for the feat of war.

Montraville therefore concluded it was impossible he should ever marry Charlotte Temple; and what end he proposed to himself by continuing the acquaintance he had commenced with her, he did not at that moment give himself time to inquire.


===Chinese===

这番话是在蒙特维尔向父亲辞行前几小时说的，因此深深印在他的脑海：于是，当贝尔库尔随他赴夏洛特的约会地点时，他便吩咐他向那法国女人打听，坦普尔小姐在财产方面可有什么指望。

小姐告诉他，坦普尔小姐的父亲虽有一份体面的自立之资，但极不可能给女儿超过一千镑；倘若她的婚事不随他的心意，他甚至可能一个子儿也不给她；而坦普尔先生也决计不会答应她嫁给一个即将启程奔赴战事的年轻人。

于是蒙特维尔断定，自己决计娶不了夏洛特·坦普尔；至于把这段已经开头的交情继续下去，他究竟图个什么结局，那一刻他也没容自己费神细想。

## XI Conflict of Love and Duty / XI 爱情与责任的交战

===Original===

Almost a week was now gone, and Charlotte continued every evening to meet Montraville, and in her heart every meeting was resolved to be the last; but alas! when Montraville, at parting, would earnestly entreat one more interview that treacherous heart betrayed her; and forgetful of its resolution, pleaded the cause of the enemy so powerfully, that Charlotte was unable to resist. Another and another meeting succeeded; and so well did Montraville improve each opportunity, that the heedless girl at length confessed no idea could be so painful to her as that of never seeing him again.

“Then we will never be parted,” said he.

“Ah, Montraville,” replied Charlotte, forcing a smile, “how can it be avoided? My parents would never consent to our union; and even could they be brought to approve of it, how should I bear to be separated from my kind, my beloved mother?”

“Then you love your parents more than you do me, Charlotte?”

===Chinese===

差不多一个星期过去了，夏洛特每晚都去见蒙特维尔，而她心里每一次都决意把这一晚当作最后一面；可是唉！每次分手时，蒙特维尔总热切地恳求再会一面，那颗不忠的心便背叛了她；它忘了自己的决心，替“敌人”说了那么有力的好话，以致夏洛特无法抗拒。一次又一次的会面接踵而来；蒙特维尔又把每个机会利用得那么好，那不留神的姑娘终于承认，再没有比永远见不到他这个念头更叫她痛苦的了。

“那我们就永远不分离。”他说。

“啊，蒙特维尔，”夏洛特强作笑容答道，“这怎么避免得了呢？我的父母决不会答应我们的结合；即便他们肯答应，我又怎么舍得离开我那慈祥的、我挚爱的母亲？”

“这么说，你爱你的父母胜过爱我，夏洛特？”

===Original===

“I hope I do,” said she, blushing and looking down; “I hope my affection for them will ever keep me from infringing the laws of filial duty.”

“Well, Charlotte,” said Montraville gravely, and letting go her hand, “since that is the case, I find I have deceived myself with fallacious hopes. I had flattered my fond heart that I was dearer to Charlotte than anything in the world besides. I thought that you would for my sake have braved the danger of the ocean, that you would, by your affection and smiles, have softened the hardships of war, and had it been my fate to fall, that your tenderness would cheer the hour of death, and smooth my passage to another world. But farewell, Charlotte! I see you never loved me. I shall now welcome the friendly ball that deprives me of the sense of my misery.”

“Oh, stay, unkind Montraville,” cried she, catching hold of his arm, as he pretended to leave her﻿—“stay; and to calm your fears, I will here protest, that were it not for the fear of giving pain to the best of parents, and returning their kindness with ingratitude, I would follow you through every danger, and in studying to promote your happiness, insure my own. But I can not break my mother’s heart, Montraville; I must not bring the gray hairs of my doting grandfather with sorrow to the grave, or make my beloved father perhaps curse the hour that gave me birth.” She covered her face with her hands and burst into tears.

===Chinese===

“我希望是这样，”她说着，红着脸垂下眼睛，“我希望我对他们的爱，永远拦着我不去触犯为子女的本分。”

“好吧，夏洛特，”蒙特维尔正色说，松开了她的手，“既然如此，我明白我是被虚妄的希望骗了。我曾让我这颗痴心自欺，以为在夏洛特心里，我比世上一切都亲。我以为你肯为我冒大海的风险，以为你的爱与笑能软化战争的艰辛；倘若我命丧沙场，你的温存会给临终的时辰添一点暖意，送我平平稳稳去往另一个世界。可是别了，夏洛特！我明白你从没爱过我。如今我只盼那颗友好的子弹快来，好叫我失去知觉，不再觉得自己的悲惨。”

“哦，别走，狠心的蒙特维尔，”他作势要走，她一把拉住他的手臂，喊道——“别走；为安你的心，我在这里立誓：若不是怕叫天下最好的父母痛心，怕以忘恩负义回报他们的恩慈，我愿追随你历尽一切险阻，在努力成全你的幸福中，成全我自己的幸福。可我不能伤我母亲的心，蒙特维尔；我决不能叫我那溺爱我的外祖父满头白发、悲悲戚戚地进了坟墓，也不能叫我挚爱的父亲也许诅咒生我的那个时辰。”她双手掩面，泪如雨下。

===Original===

“All these distressing scenes, my dear Charlotte,” cried Montraville, “are merely the chimeras of a disturbed fancy. Your parents might perhaps grieve at first, but when they heard from your own hands that you were with a man of honor, and that it was to insure your felicity by an union with him, to which you feared they would never have given their assent, that you left their protection, they will, be assured, forgive an error which love alone occasioned, and when we return from America, receive you with open arms and tears of joy.”

Belcour and mademoiselle heard this last speech, and conceiving it a proper time to throw in their advice and persuasions, approached Charlotte, and so well seconded the entreaties of Montraville, that finding mademoiselle intended going with Belcour, and feeling her own treacherous heart too much inclined to accompany them, the hapless Charlotte in an evil hour consented that the next evening they should bring a chaise to the end of the town, and that she would leave her friends, and throw herself entirely on the protection of Montraville. “But should you,” said she, looking earnestly at him, her eyes full of tears, “should you, forgetful of your promises, and repenting the engagements you here voluntarily enter into, forsake and leave me on a foreign shore﻿—”

“Judge not so meanly of me,” said he. “The moment we reach our place of destination, Hymen shall sanctify our love, and when I shall forget your goodness may Heaven forget me!”

===Chinese===

“这些凄惨的情景，我亲爱的夏洛特，”蒙特维尔喊道，“不过是心神纷乱时想出来的怪物。你的父母起初或许会悲伤；可当他们亲耳听你讲明，你跟的是一个有名誉的人，你离开他们的护翼，正是为了用一桩你怕他们永远不肯应允的婚姻来确保你的幸福，那么请相信，他们会原谅这个只因爱情而起的过失；等我们从美洲回来，他们会张开双臂、含着欢喜的泪迎接你。”

这最后一席话被贝尔库尔和小姐听见了，两人觉得这正是进言相劝的好时机，便走近夏洛特，又那么卖力地帮腔蒙特维尔的恳求，结果，眼看小姐打算同贝尔库尔同去，又觉得自己那颗不忠的心太想随他们同去，不幸的夏洛特在厄运降临的一刻竟答应了：第二天傍晚他们把马车赶到镇梢，她将离开她的朋友们，把自己完全托付给蒙特维尔的保护。“可要是你，”她说，泪眼盈盈地凝视着他，“要是你忘了你的诺言，后悔你在这里自愿许下的盟约，把我抛弃在异国的海岸上——”

“不要这样小看我，”他说。“我们一到目的地，婚姻之神就会祝圣我们的爱情；我若忘了你的恩情，愿上天忘掉我！”

===Original===

“Ah,” said Charlotte, leaning on mademoiselle’s arm, as they walked up the garden together, “I have forgot all that I ought to have remembered, in consenting to this intended elopement.”

“You are a strange girl,” said mademoiselle: “you never know your own mind two minutes at a time. Just now you declared Montraville’s happiness was what you prized most in the world; and now I suppose you repent having insured that happiness by agreeing to accompany him abroad.”

“Indeed, I do repent,” replied Charlotte, “from my soul; but while discretion points out the impropriety of my conduct, inclination urges me on to ruin.”

===Chinese===

“唉，”两人一路走上花园时，夏洛特倚着小姐的手臂说，“答应了这桩私奔，我把我该记住的全忘光了。”

“你真是个奇怪的姑娘，”小姐说，“从来连两分钟都拿不定自己的主意。方才你还宣称，蒙特维尔的幸福是你在这世上最看重的；现在呢，我猜你又后悔用答应陪他出洋的办法保住那份幸福了。”

“我真的后悔，”夏洛特答道，“打心底里后悔；可理智指着我行为的失当，情偏好偏推着我走向毁灭。”

===Original===

“Ruin! fiddlesticks!” said mademoiselle; “am not I going with you? and do I feel any of these qualms?”

“You do not renounce a tender father and mother,” said Charlotte.

“But I hazard my dear reputation,” replied mademoiselle, bridling.

“True,” replied Charlotte, “but you do not feel what I do.” She then bade her good night: but sleep was a stranger to her eyes, and the tear of anguish watered her pillow.


===Chinese===

“毁灭！胡说八道！”小姐说，“我不是也跟你们一起去吗？我可有你这些心惊肉跳？”

“你抛弃的可不是慈爱的父亲和母亲。”夏洛特说。

“可我拿我宝贝的名声冒险呢，”小姐昂着头答道。

“那倒是，”夏洛特答道，“可你感受不到我的感受。”随后她道了晚安：但睡意与她无缘，苦痛的泪浸湿了她的枕头。

## XII “How Thou Art Fall’n!” / XII “你堕落得何等深重！”

===Original===

Nature’s last, best gift:
						

						Creature in whom excell’d, whatever could
						

						To sight or thought be nam’d!
						

						Holy, divine! good, amiable, and sweet!
						

						How thou art fall’n!﻿—﻿—﻿

When Charlotte left her restless bed, her languid eye and pale cheek discovered to Madame Du Pont the little repose she had tasted.

===Chinese===

造物最后的、最好的馈赠：

你这受造之物啊，凡可言说于

目光与思想之前的万有，无出其右！

神圣，圣洁！善良，可亲，又甜美！

你堕落得何等深重！——

夏洛特离开她辗转反侧的床榻时，那无神的眼、苍白的颊，已向迪蓬夫人泄露了她所得到的那一点安眠。

===Original===

“My dear child,” said the affectionate governess, “what is the cause of the langor so apparent in your frame? Are you not well?”

“Yes, my dear madame, very well,” replied Charlotte, attempting to smile, “but I know not how it was; I could not sleep last night, and my spirits are depressed this morning.”

“Come, cheer up, my love,” said the governess; “I believe I have brought a cordial to revive them. I have just received a letter from your good mamma, and here is one for yourself.”

Charlotte hastily took the letter: it contained these words﻿—

===Chinese===

“我亲爱的孩子，”慈爱的女管家说，“你身上这明显的委顿，是什么缘故？你不舒服么？”

“没有，亲爱的夫人，很好，”夏洛特答道，勉强笑了笑，“可不知怎么的；昨夜我睡不着，今早精神又很消沉。”

“来，高兴起来，我的宝贝，”女管家说，“我相信我带来了一剂提神的心药。我刚收到你善良的妈妈的信，这里还有一封是给你的。”

夏洛特急急接过信：信上是这样写的——

===Original===

“As tomorrow is the anniversary of the happy day that gave my beloved girl to the anxious wishes of a maternal heart, I have requested your governess to let you come home and spend it with us; and as I know you to be a good, affectionate child, and make it your study to improve in those branches of education which you know will give most pleasure to your delighted parents, as a reward for your diligence and attention, I have prepared an agreeable surprise for your reception. Your grandfather, eager to embrace the darling of his aged heart, will come in the chaise for you; so hold yourself in readiness to attend him by nine o’clock. Your dear father joins in every tender wish for your health and future felicity which warms the heart of my dear Charlotte’s affectionate mother.

“L. Temple.”

“Gracious Heaven!” cried Charlotte, forgetting where she was, and raising her streaming eyes as in earnest supplication.

Madame Du Pont was surprised. “Why these tears, my love?” said she. “Why this seeming agitation? I thought the letter would have rejoiced, instead of distressing you.”

===Chinese===

“明天是那个幸福日子的周年，那一天把我亲爱的姑娘交到了一颗母亲焦盼的心怀；我已请求你的女管家放你回家，同我们一起度过这一天；我深知你是个善良孝顺的孩子，一向勤于研习那些你知道最能让欣喜的父母高兴的功课；为奖赏你的勤奋与用心，我为你准备了一个称心的惊喜。你的外祖父急着要拥抱他暮年的心肝宝贝，会亲自赶车来接你；所以你收拾停当，九点钟随他动身。你亲爱的父亲同我一起，把每一份为你健康和未来幸福的祝愿，都寄予我亲爱的夏洛特的慈母之心。

“露·坦普尔（L. Temple）”

“慈悲的上天啊！”夏洛特喊出声来，忘了自己身在何处，泪眼里抬起眼睛，像是在恳切祈求。

迪蓬夫人吃了一惊。“怎么流眼泪了，我的宝贝？”她说。“这般的激动却是为什么？我还以为这封信会让你欢喜，而不是叫你难过呢。”

===Original===

“It does rejoice me,” replied Charlotte, endeavoring at composure; “but I was praying for merit to deserve the unremitted attentions of the best of parents.”

“You do right,” said Madame Du Pont, “to ask the assistance of Heaven that you may continue to deserve their love. Continue, my dear Charlotte, in the course you have ever pursued, and you will insure at once their happiness and your own.”

“Oh!” cried Charlotte, as her governess left her, “I have forfeited both forever! Yet let me reflect:﻿—the irrevocable step is not yet taken: it is not too late to recede from the brink of a precipice from which I can only behold the dark abyss of ruin, shame and remorse!”

===Chinese===

“我确实欢喜，”夏洛特答道，竭力镇定下来，“我方才是在祈求上天赐我德行，叫我配得上最好的父母无微不至的关爱。”

“你做得对，”迪蓬夫人说，“求上天帮助，好叫你继续配得上他们的爱。照着你一贯走的路走下去吧，我亲爱的夏洛特，你就能同时确保他们的幸福和你自己的幸福。”

“哦！”女管家走后，夏洛特喊道，“这两样我都永远失掉了！可让我想想：——那无可挽回的一步还没有迈出：现在从悬崖边上退回来还不算晚，从那崖边，我望见的只有毁灭、耻辱与悔恨的黑暗深渊！”

===Original===

She arose from her seat, and flew to the apartment of La Rue. “Oh, mademoiselle!” said she, “I am snatched by a miracle from destruction! This letter has saved me: it has opened my eyes to the folly I was so near committing. I will not go, mademoiselle; I will not wound the hearts of those dear parents who make my happiness the whole study of their lives.”

“Well,” said mademoiselle, “do as you please, miss; but pray understand that my resolution is taken, and it is not in your power to alter it. I shall meet the gentlemen at the appointed hour, and shall not be surprised at any outrage which Montraville may commit when he finds himself disappointed. Indeed, I should not be astonished were he to come immediately here and reproach you for your instability in the hearing of the whole school: and what will be the consequence? You will bear the odium of having formed the resolution of eloping, and every girl of spirit will laugh at your want of fortitude to put it in execution, while prudes and fools will load you with reproach and contempt. You will have lost the confidence of your parents, incurred their anger and the scoffs of the world; and what fruit do you expect to reap from this piece of heroism, (for such, no doubt, you think it is)? You will have the pleasure to reflect that you have deceived the man who adores you, and whom, in your heart, you prefer to all other men, and that you are separated from him forever.”

This eloquent harangue was given with such volubility that Charlotte could not find an opportunity to interrupt her or to offer a single word till the whole was finished, and then found her ideas so confused that she knew not what to say.

===Chinese===

她从座位上站起来，飞奔到拉吕的房里。“哦，小姐！”她说，“我被一个奇迹从毁灭里救出来了！这封信救了我：它让我看清了我险些犯下的愚蠢。我不去了，小姐；我不去伤那两位亲爱的父母的心了——他们毕生所营，就是我的幸福。”

“好吧，”小姐说，“随你的便吧，小姐；可是请你明白，我的决心已经定了，你无力更改。我会按时去赴那两位先生的约；蒙特维尔发现自己落了空时会有什么过激的举动，我一概不会奇怪。真的，他要是立刻赶到这儿来，当着全校的面数落你的反复无常，我也不会吃惊：那会怎么样呢？你将背上下决心私奔的恶名，每个有胆量的姑娘都会笑话你没本事把决心付诸实行，而道学先生和蠢货们更要把你骂个狗血淋头。你将失去父母的信任，惹他们动怒，还要受世人的讥嘲；那么你指望从这番英雄行为（因为你无疑正是这么看的）里收获什么果子呢？你将得到一份快慰：你欺骗了那个崇拜你的男人——而在你心底，你爱他胜过一切男子——并且从此同他永别。”

这一番滔滔雄辩说得那样连珠不停，夏洛特根本找不到插一句话的机会，直到全篇说完；而这时她的思绪又乱作一团，不知说什么好。

===Original===

At length she determined that she would go with mademoiselle to the place of assignation, convince Montraville of the necessity of adhering to the resolution of remaining behind, assure him of her affection, and bid him adieu.

Charlotte formed this plan in her mind, and exulted in the certainty of its success. “How shall I rejoice,” said she, “in this triumph of reason over inclination; and when in the arms of my affectionate parents, lift up my soul in gratitude to Heaven as I look back on the dangers I have escaped!”

The hour of assignation arrived: mademoiselle put what money and valuables she possessed in her pocket, and advised Charlotte to do the same; but she refused; “my resolution is fixed;” said she; “I will sacrifice love to duty.”

===Chinese===

最后她拿定主意：随小姐到约会的地方去，让蒙特维尔明白她必须坚守留下的决心不可，向他保证她的爱，然后与他诀别。

夏洛特在心里这样盘算着，为它必然的成功而暗自欢喜。“我将怎样欢庆，”她说，“这理性对情欲的胜利；当我投入慈爱的父母怀中，回望我脱险的这一路，我又将怎样向天举起感恩的灵魂！”

约会的时辰到了：小姐把自己所有的钱财细软装进口袋，也劝夏洛特照样做；她却拒绝了；“我的决心已定；”她说；“我愿为责任牺牲爱情。”

===Original===

Mademoiselle smiled internally; and they proceeded softly down the back stairs and out of the garden gate. Montraville and Belcour were ready to receive them.

“Now,” said Montraville, taking Charlotte in his arms, “you are mine forever.”

“No,” said she, withdrawing from his embrace; “I am come to take an everlasting farewell.”

It would be useless to repeat the conversation that here ensued; suffice it to say, that Montraville used every argument that had formerly been successful, Charlotte’s resolution began to waver, and he drew her almost imperceptibly toward the chaise.

===Chinese===

小姐心里暗暗发笑；两人轻轻下了后楼梯，出了园门。蒙特维尔和贝尔库尔已候在那里。

“好了，”蒙特维尔把夏洛特揽进怀里，说，“你永远是我的了。”

“不，”她说，从他怀抱里挣脱出来，“我是来道永别的。”

这里接下来的谈话，重复一遍也无益；只需说一句：蒙特维尔把先前奏效过的论证又用了个遍，夏洛特的决心开始动摇，他几乎不知不觉地把她引向了马车。

===Original===

“I can not go,” said she, “cease, dear Montraville, to persuade. I must not: religion, duty, forbid.”

“Cruel Charlotte!” said he, “if you disappoint my ardent hopes, by all that is sacred! this hand shall put a period to my existence. I can not﻿—will not﻿—live without you.”

“Alas! my torn heart!” said Charlotte, “how shall I act?”

“Let me direct you,” said Montraville, lifting her into the chaise.

===Chinese===

“我不能去，”她说，“别再劝了，亲爱的蒙特维尔。我决不能去：宗教、责任，都不容许。”

“狠心的夏洛特！”他说，“你若辜负我炽热的期望，我对着一切神圣起誓！这只手就结果了我的性命。没有你，我不能——也不愿——活。”

“唉！我这颗撕裂的心！”夏洛特说，“我该怎么办？”

“让我来指引你。”蒙特维尔说着，把她抱进了马车。

===Original===

“Oh! my dear forsaken parents!” cried Charlotte.

The chaise drove off. She shrieked and fainted into the arms of her betrayer.


===Chinese===

“哦！我亲爱的被舍弃的父母啊！”夏洛特喊道。

马车驶动了。她一声尖呼，晕倒在她的诱骗者怀里。

## XIII Cruel Disappointment / XIII 残忍的失望

===Original===

“What pleasure!” cried Mr. Eldridge, as he stepped into the chaise to go for his granddaughter, “what pleasure expands the heart of an old man when he beholds the progeny of a beloved child growing up in every virtue that adorned the minds of her parents. I foolishly thought, some few years since, that every sense of joy was buried in the graves of my dear partner and my son; but my Lucy, by her filial affection, soothed my soul to peace, and this dear Charlotte has twined herself round my heart, and opened such new scenes of delight to my view that I almost forget I have ever been unhappy.”

When the chaise stopped he alighted with the alacrity of youth; so much do the emotions of the soul influence the body.

It was half-past eight o’clock: the ladies were assembled in the schoolroom, and Madame Du Pont was preparing to offer the morning sacrifice of prayer and praise, when it was discovered that mademoiselle and Charlotte were missing.

“She is busy, no doubt,” said the governess, “in preparing Charlotte for her little excursion; but pleasure should never make us forget our duty to our Creator. Go, one of you, and bid them both attend prayers.”

===Chinese===

“多么快活！”埃尔德里奇先生跨进马车、要去接外孙女时喊道，“一个老人眼见爱女的后代渐渐长成，兼具她父母当年装饰心灵的种种美德，他的心里便涨开了多么大的欢喜！几年前我还愚蠢地以为，每一分欢愉都已随我亲爱的老伴和儿子埋进了坟墓；可是我的露西以她的孝心，把我的灵魂抚慰得安宁了，而这个亲爱的夏洛特又缠绕上了我的心，为我展开一片片新的乐境，我几乎忘了我曾经不幸过。”

马车停稳，他以年轻人的轻快跳下车来；心灵的情绪就是这样左右着身体。

那时是八点半钟：女士们已齐聚在课室里，迪蓬夫人正准备献上晨间祈祷与赞美的祭礼，忽然发现小姐和夏洛特不见了。

“她准是在忙，”女管家说，“替夏洛特打点短途的行装；但快乐决不该使我们忘记对造物主的本分。去一个人，叫她们两个都来做祷告。”

===Original===

The lady who went to summon them soon returned, and informed the governess that the room was locked, and that she had knocked repeatedly, but obtained no answer.

“Good Heaven!” cried Madame Du Pont, “this is very strange:” and turning pale with terror, she went hastily to the door, and ordered it to be forced open. The apartment instantly discovered the fact that no person had been in it the preceding night, the beds appearing as though just made. The house was instantly a scene of confusion: the garden, the pleasure grounds, were searched to no purpose; every apartment rang with the names of Miss Temple and mademoiselle; but they were too distant to hear; and every face wore the marks of disappointment.

Mr. Eldridge was sitting in the parlor, eagerly expecting his granddaughter to descend, ready equipped for her journey: he heard the confusion that reigned in the house; he heard the name of Charlotte frequently repeated.

“What can be the matter?” said he, rising, and opening the door: “I fear some accident has befallen my dear girl.”

===Chinese===

去叫的那位女士很快回来了，禀告女管家说，房门锁着，她敲了好几遍，无人应答。

“天哪！”迪蓬夫人喊道，“这太奇怪了：”她吓得脸色发白，急忙赶到门口，命人把门撬开。屋里的情形立刻表明，前一夜没有任何人在里面睡过，两张床整齐得像刚铺好的一样。整座宅子顿时乱作一团：花园、园圃都搜遍了，一无所获；每一个房间里都回响着坦普尔小姐和小姐的名字；但她们早已走远，听不见了；每一张脸上都挂着失望。

埃尔德里奇先生坐在客厅里，急切地盼着外孙女收拾停当、下楼启程：他听得满屋纷乱；他听得夏洛特的名字被频频呼唤。

“出了什么事？”他说着，站起来开了门：“我怕是我亲爱的姑娘遭了什么意外。”

===Original===

The governess entered. The visible agitation of her countenance discovered that something extraordinary had happened.

“Where is Charlotte?” said he. “Why does not my child come to welcome her doting parent?”

“Be composed, my dear sir,” said Madame Du Pont; “do not frighten yourself unnecessarily. She is not in the house at present; but as mademoiselle is undoubtedly with her, she will speedily return in safety; and I hope they will both be able to account for this unseasonable absence in such a manner as shall remove our present uneasiness.”

“Madame,” cried the old man, with an angry look, “has my child been accustomed to go out without leave, with no other company or protector than that French woman? Pardon me, madame, I mean no reflections on your country, but I never did like Mademoiselle La Rue; I think she was a very improper person to be entrusted with the care of such a girl as Charlotte Temple, or to be suffered to take her from under your immediate protection.”

===Chinese===

女管家走了进来。她脸上明显的激动表明发生了不寻常的事。

“夏洛特在哪里？”他说。“我的孩子怎么不来迎接她疼爱她的外祖父？”

“请镇定，亲爱的先生，”迪蓬夫人说；“不要无端惊吓自己。她此刻不在宅子里；但小姐既是同她在一起，想必很快就会平安回来；我希望她们俩都能把这趟不合时宜的外出解释清楚，好解除我们眼下的不安。”

“夫人，”老人怒容满面地喊道，“难道我的孩子惯于不经准许就外出，除了那个法国女人，再无别的同伴和保护人？原谅我，夫人，我并非有意贬抑贵国，可我从来就不喜欢拉吕小姐；我想，把照管夏洛特·坦普尔这样一个姑娘的重任托付给她，或是容她把这样的姑娘从您的直接保护之下带走，本来就是极不合适的。”

===Original===

“You wrong me, Mr. Eldridge,” replied she, “if you suppose I have ever permitted your granddaughter to go out, unless with the other ladies. I would to Heaven I could form any probable conjecture concerning her absence this morning, but it is a mystery which her return can alone unravel.”

Servants were now dispatched to every place where there was the least hope of hearing any tidings of the fugitives, but in vain. Dreadful were the hours of horrid suspense which Mr. Eldridge passed till twelve o’clock, when that suspense was reduced to a shocking certainty, and every spark of hope, which till then they had indulged, was in a moment extinguished.

Mr. Eldridge was preparing, with a heavy heart, to return to his anxiously-expecting children, when Madame Du Pont received the following note without either name or date:

“Miss Temple is well, and wishes to relieve the anxiety of her parents, by letting them know she has voluntarily put herself under the protection of a man whose future study shall be to make her happy. Pursuit is needless; the measures taken to avoid discovery are too effectual to be eluded. When she thinks her friends are reconciled to this precipitate step, they may, perhaps, be informed of her place of residence. Mademoiselle is with her.”

===Chinese===

“您冤枉我了，埃尔德里奇先生，”她答道，“倘若您以为我曾准许您的外孙女出门，除非同其他女士们一起。但愿上天保佑，我能对她今晨的失踪作个像样的推测；可这是个谜，只有等她回来才能解开。”

仆人们被打发到每一个还存着一线希望、或可探得逃亡者音讯的地方，却是徒劳。埃尔德里奇先生在骇人的悬虑中度过的那几个小时真是可怕，直到十二点钟，那悬虑化成了骇人的确讯，他们此前一直纵容自己的每一星希望，顷刻间熄灭了。

埃尔德里奇先生正怀着沉重的心，准备回到焦望他的儿女那里去，迪蓬夫人收到了下面这封既无署名又无日期的短简：

“坦普尔小姐安好，并愿解除父母的悬虑，让他们知道：她已自愿将自己置于一位男子的保护之下，此人今后的志趣，便是使她幸福。追寻是无益的；为免人觉察而采取的手段十分周密，无从躲避。待她认为亲友们已原谅了这仓促的一步，或许会让他们得知她的住处。小姐与她同在。”

===Original===

As Madame Du Pont read these cruel lines, she turned pale as ashes, her limbs trembled, and she was forced to call for a glass of water. She loved Charlotte truly; and when she reflected on the innocence and gentleness of her disposition, she concluded that it must have been the advice and machinations of La Rue which led her to this imprudent action; she recollected her agitation at the receipt of her mother’s letter, and saw in it the conflict of her mind.

“Does that letter relate to Charlotte?” said Mr. Eldridge, having waited some time in expectation of Madame Du Pont’s speaking.

“It does,” said she. “Charlotte is well, but can not return today.”

“Not return, madame? Where is she? Who will detain her from her fond, expecting parents?”

===Chinese===

迪蓬夫人读着这些残酷的字句，面如死灰，四肢发抖，只得叫人端一杯水来。她真心疼爱夏洛特；想到她性情的纯真温婉，她断定必是拉吕的教唆和阴谋把她引向了这轻率的举动；她记起她收到母亲信时的激动，从中看出了她内心的交战。

“那封信是说夏洛特么？”埃尔德里奇先生说，他已等候多时，只当迪蓬夫人要开口了。

“正是。”她说。“夏洛特安好，只是今天回不来了。”

“回不来，夫人？她在哪里？谁扣住了她，不让她回到疼爱她、盼着她的父母身边？”

===Original===

“You distract me with these questions, Mr. Eldridge. Indeed, I know not where she is, or who has seduced her from her duty.”

The whole truth now rushed at once upon Mr. Eldridge’s mind. “She has eloped, then,” said he: “my child is betrayed; the darling, the comfort of my aged heart is lost! Oh, would to heaven I had died but yesterday.”

A violent gush of grief in some measure relieved him, and, after several vain attempts, he at length assumed sufficient composure to read the note.

“And how shall I return to my children?” said he: “how approach that mansion so late the habitation of peace? Alas! my dear Lucy, how will you support these heartrending tidings? or how shall I be enabled to console you, who need so much consolation myself?”

The old man returned to the chaise, but the light step and cheerful countenance were no more; sorrow filled his heart and guided his emotions.

He seated himself in the chaise; his venerable head reclined upon his bosom, his hands were folded, his eye fixed on vacancy, and the large drops of sorrow rolled silently down his cheeks. There was a mixture of anguish and resignation depicted in his countenance, as if he would say:

“Henceforth, who shall dare to boast his happiness, or even in idea contemplate his treasure, lest in the very moment his heart is exulting in its own felicity, the object which constitutes that felicity should be torn from him?”


===Chinese===

“您这些问题叫我心乱，埃尔德里奇先生。真的，我不知道她在哪里，也不知道是谁把她从本分上诱走的。”

整个真相此刻一下子涌上埃尔德里奇先生的心头。“那么她是私奔了，”他说：“我的孩子被出卖了；这个宝贝、我这暮年的安慰，丢了！哦，但愿我昨天就死了。”

一阵激烈的悲恸多少纾解了他；几番徒然的努力之后，他终于强自镇定，把那封短简读完了。

“可我怎么回去见我的儿女？”他说：“怎么走进那座不久前还安住着安宁的宅子？唉！我亲爱的露西，这样撕心的消息你怎受得住？我自己尚且需要如此多的安慰，又哪来的力量安慰你？”

老人回到马车上，可那轻快的脚步和欢悦的容颜都不见了；悲伤填满他的心，左右着他的情绪。

他在车里坐定；那可敬的头颅垂到胸前，双手交叠，双眼凝望着虚空，大颗大颗的泪珠顺着面颊无声滚落。他脸上交织着痛苦与顺从，仿佛在说：

“从今往后，谁还敢夸耀自己的幸福，谁还敢哪怕在念头里细数自己的珍宝，惟恐正当他的心为自己的福乐扬扬得意之际，那构成这福乐的对象会被从他手中夺走？”

## XIV Maternal Sorrow / XIV 为母的悲伤

===Original===

Slow and heavy passed the time while the carriage was conveying Mr. Eldridge home; and yet, when he came in sight of the house, he wished a longer reprieve from the dreadful task of informing Mr. and Mrs. Temple of their daughter’s elopement.

It is easy to judge the anxiety of these affectionate parents, when they found the return of their father delayed so much beyond the expected time. They were now met in the dining parlor, and several of the young people who had been invited were already arrived. Each different part of the company was employed in the same manner, looking out at the windows which faced the road. At length the long-expected chaise appeared. Mrs. Temple ran out to receive and welcome her darling: her young companions flocked around the door, each one eager to give her joy on the return of her birthday. The door of the chaise was opened. Charlotte was not there. “Where is my child?” cried Mrs. Temple, in breathless agitation. Mr. Eldridge could not answer; he took hold of his daughter’s hand and led her into the house; and sinking on the first chair he came to, burst into tears, and sobbed aloud.

“She is dead!” cried Mrs. Temple. “Oh, my dear Charlotte?” and, clasping her hands in an agony of distress, fell into strong hysterics.

Mr. Temple, who had stood speechless with surprise and fear, now ventured to enquire if indeed his Charlotte was no more. Mr. Eldridge led him into another apartment; and putting the fatal note into his hand, cried: “Bear it like a Christian!” and turned from him, endeavoring to suppress his own too visible emotions.

===Chinese===

马车载着埃尔德里奇先生回家的路上，时光过得又慢又沉重；然而，当他望见宅子的时候，却又巴望能再缓一缓，晚一点去面对那可怕的任务——把女儿私奔的消息告诉坦普尔先生和坦普尔夫人。

这两位慈爱的父母见父亲归来的时辰比预计的迟了这么多，其焦灼可想而知。他们此刻正聚在餐室里，应邀而来的几位年轻人也已经到了。每一处的来客都在做同一件事：凭窗眺望那条大路。终于，久盼的马车出现了。坦普尔夫人跑出去迎接、欢迎她的宝贝：她的小伙伴们围拢在门口，个个急着要在她生日归来时向她道喜。车厢门开了。夏洛特不在里面。“我的孩子在哪里？”坦普尔夫人喊道，激动得喘不过气来。埃尔德里奇先生答不上话；他拉起女儿的手，把她领进屋里；跌坐在遇到的第一把椅子上，泪如泉涌，失声呜咽。

“她死了！”坦普尔夫人喊道。“哦，我亲爱的夏洛特！”她悲愤至极，绞着双手，一阵剧烈的歇斯底里发作起来。

坦普尔先生先是惊惧得说不出话，此刻才敢动问，他的夏洛特是否真的没了。埃尔德里奇先生把他领进另一间屋子；把那封致命的短简放进他手里，喊道：“像个基督徒那样承受吧！”便转过身去，竭力按捺自己那过于外露的情绪。

===Original===

It would be vain to attempt describing what Mr. Temple felt whilst he hastily ran over the dreadful lines. When he had finished, the paper dropped from his unnerved hand. “Gracious Heaven!” said he, “could Charlotte act thus?” Neither tear nor sigh escaped him; and he sat the image of mute sorrow, till roused from his stupor by the repeated shrieks of Mrs. Temple. He rose hastily, and rushing into the apartment where she was, folded his arms about her, and saying﻿—“Let us be patient, my dear Lucy,” nature relieved his almost bursting heart by a friendly gush of tears.

Should anyone, presuming on his own philosophic temper, look with an eye of contempt on the man who could indulge a woman’s weakness, let him remember that man was a father, and he will then pity the misery which wrung those drops from a noble, generous heart.

Mrs. Temple, beginning to be a little more composed, but still imagining her child was dead, her husband, gently taking her hand, cried: “You are mistaken, my love. Charlotte is not dead.”

“Then she is very ill; else why did she not come? But I will go to her; the chaise is still at the door; let me go instantly to the dear girl. If I was ill, she would fly to attend me, to alleviate my sufferings, and cheer me with her love.”

===Chinese===

坦普尔先生匆匆扫过那些可怕的字句时心里是什么滋味，描摹也是徒劳。读罢，那张纸从他无力的手中滑落。“慈悲的上天！”他说，“夏洛特会做出这种事来么？”他没有流泪，也没有叹息；他坐在那里，成了一尊无声悲恸的雕像，直到坦普尔夫人一声声的尖叫把他从麻木中惊醒。他霍地站起来，冲进她所在的房间，双臂把她搂住，说了一句——“要忍耐，我亲爱的露西，”天性便以一阵友好的热泪，纾解了他那几乎胀裂的心。

倘若有人仗着自己哲学家的涵养，要轻蔑地看这纵容妇人软弱的男人，那就请他记住：这男人是一位父亲；那时他便会怜悯那从一个高贵慷慨的心中绞出这些泪珠的苦痛了。

坦普尔夫人渐渐平静了一些，却仍当她的孩子死了；她的丈夫轻轻握住她的手，喊道：“你想岔了，我的爱人。夏洛特没有死。”

“那她就是病得很重；不然她怎么不来？我要去看她；马车还在门口；让我即刻到那亲爱的姑娘那里去。我若是病了，她会飞来伺候我，减轻我的苦楚，用她的爱为我打气。”

===Original===

“Be calm, my dearest Lucy, and I will tell you all,” said Mr. Temple. “You must not go; indeed you must not; it will be of no use.”

“Temple,” said she, assuming a look of firmness and composure, “tell me the truth, I beseech you! I can not bear this dreadful suspense. What misfortune has befallen my child? Let me know the worst, and I will endeavor to bear it as I ought.”

“Lucy,” replied Mr. Temple, “imagine your daughter alive, and in no danger of death: what misfortune would you then dread?”

“There is one misfortune which is worse than death. But I know my child too well to suspect﻿—”

===Chinese===

“镇静些，我最亲爱的露西，我把一切都告诉你，”坦普尔先生说。“你不能去；真的不能去；去了也没有用。”

“坦普尔，”她说，摆出坚毅镇定的神情，“告诉我实话，我求你！这可怕的悬念我受不住了。我的孩子遭了什么不幸？让我知道最坏的，我会尽力照我应该的那样承受。”

“露西，”坦普尔先生答道，“设想你的女儿活着，也没有性命之虞：那时你还会怕什么不幸？”

“有一种不幸比死还坏。可我太了解我的孩子，绝不会疑心——”

===Original===

“Be not too confident, Lucy.”

“Oh, Heavens!” said she, “what horrid images do you start? Is it possible she should forget?”

“She has forgot us all, my love; she has preferred the love of a stranger to the affectionate protection of her friends.”

“Not eloped!” cried she, eagerly.

===Chinese===

“别太自信，露西。”

“哦，老天！”她说，“你起了多么可怕的念头！难道她会忘了？”

“她把我们全忘了，我的爱人；她把一个陌生人的爱，看得比亲友们的慈爱保护还重。”

“不是私奔了吧！”她急切地喊道。

===Original===

Mr. Temple was silent.

“You can not contradict it,” said she. “I see my fate in those tearful eyes. Oh, Charlotte! Charlotte! how ill have you requited our tenderness! But, Father of Mercies,” continued she, sinking on her knees and raising her streaming eyes and clasped hands to Heaven, “this once vouchsafe to hear a fond, a distracted mother’s prayer. Oh, let thy bounteous Providence watch over and protect the dear, thoughtless girl, save her from the miseries which I fear will be her portion; and, oh! of Thine infinite mercy, make her not a mother, lest she should one day feel what I now suffer!”

The last words faltered on her tongue, and she fell fainting into the arms of her husband, who had involuntarily dropped on his knees beside her.

===Chinese===

坦普尔先生沉默了。

“你无法否认，”她说。“我从你含泪的眼睛里看到了我的命运。哦，夏洛特！夏洛特！你何等辜负了我们的疼爱！可是，慈悲的天父啊，”她接着说，跪倒在地，含泪的眼睛和紧握的双手举向上天，“这一次，请垂听一个痴心的、心乱如焚的母亲的祈祷吧。哦，愿你博施的上天看顾和保护那个亲爱的、不知愁的姑娘，救她脱离我怕将成为她份内的苦难；哦！以你无限的慈悲，别让她做母亲吧，免得她有一天尝到我此刻的痛苦！”

最后几个字在她舌尖上颤着消散了，她晕倒在她丈夫的怀里——他早已不由自主地跪在了她的身旁。

===Original===

A mother’s anguish, when disappointed in her tenderest hopes, none but a mother can conceive. Yet, my dear young readers, I would have you read this scene with attention, and reflect that you may yourselves one day be mothers.

Oh, my friends, as you value your eternal happiness, wound not, by thoughtless ingratitude, the peace of the mother who bore you: remember the tenderness, the care, the unremitting anxiety with which she has attended to all your wants and wishes from earliest infancy to the present day; behold the mild ray of affectionate applause that beams from her eye on the performance of your duty: listen to her reproofs with silent attention; they proceed from a heart anxious for your future felicity: you must love her; nature, all-powerful nature, has planted the seeds of filial affection in your bosoms.

Then once more read over the sorrows of poor Mrs. Temple; and remember, the mother whom you so dearly love and venerate will feel the same, when you, forgetful of the respect due to your Maker and yourself, forsake the paths of virtue, for those of vice and folly.


===Chinese===

一个母亲在至柔的希望破灭时的绞痛，只有做母亲的才能想见。然而，我亲爱的年轻读者们，我愿你们细读这一幕，并且想一想，你们自己有一天也会做母亲。

哦，我的朋友们，既然你们看重自己永恒的幸福，就决不要以轻率的忘恩，去伤害生你们的母亲心中的安宁：记住她从你们襁褓之时直到今日，怀着怎样的温柔、怎样的操劳、怎样无休止的牵挂，来照应你们的一切需要和愿望；看她履行本分时，她眼中映出的那道嘉许的柔光；以默默的专心听她的责备；那是出自一颗挂虑你们未来幸福的心：你们必须爱她；全能的自然，早把孝心的种子播进了你们的胸中。

然后，再把可怜的坦普尔夫人的悲伤重读一遍；并且记住，当你们忘了对自己造物主、对自己应尽的敬重，弃美德之路而就罪恶与愚妄之路时，你们如此深爱、如此敬奉的母亲，也会有一模一样的感受。

## XV Embarkation / XV 登船启航

===Original===

It was with the utmost difficulty that the united efforts of mademoiselle and Montraville could support Charlotte’s spirits during their short ride from Chichester7 to Portsmouth, where a boat waited to take them immediately on board the ship in which they were to embark for America.

As soon as she became tolerably composed, she entreated pen and ink to write to her parents. This she did in the most affecting, artless manner, entreating their pardon and blessing, and describing the dreadful situation of her mind, the conflict she suffered in endeavoring to conquer this unfortunate attachment, and concluded with saying her only hope of future comfort consisted in the (perhaps delusive) idea she indulged, of being once more folded in their protecting arms, and hearing the words of peace and pardon from their lips.

The tears streamed incessantly while she was writing, and she was frequently obliged to lay down her pen: but when the task was completed, and she had committed the letter to the care of Montraville, to be sent to the post office, she became more calm, and indulging the delightful hope of soon receiving an answer that would seal her pardon, she in some measure assumed her usual cheerfulness.

===Chinese===

从奇切斯特[7]到朴次茅斯这一短短的旅程中，全靠小姐和蒙特维尔合力宽解，夏洛特的精神才勉强支撑得住；朴次茅斯有一艘小艇等着，要把她们立刻送上那艘即将启航开赴美洲的船。

她刚一勉强平静下来，便恳求给她纸笔，给父母写信。她写得那样动人、那样毫无心机：恳求他们的宽恕和祝福，描画她心境的可怕，描画她努力克服这段不幸情缘所受的煎熬，末了说，她对来日安慰的惟一指望，全在于她纵容自己的那个（也许是虚妄的）念头——有朝一日重被父母护佑的双臂搂住，亲耳听他们说出和平与宽恕的话。

写信时她泪流不止，好几回不得不搁下笔来：但写完了，把信托付给蒙特维尔、让他送去邮局之后，她平静了些，纵着自己去想那很快便能得到封印宽恕的回音的甜美希望，多少恢复了往日的欢颜。

===Original===

But Montraville knew too well the consequences that must unavoidably ensue should this letter reach Mr. Temple: he, therefore, wisely resolved to walk on the deck, tear it in pieces, and commit the fragments to the care of Neptune, who might or might not, as it suited his convenience, convey them on shore.

All Charlotte’s hopes and wishes were now concentered in one, namely, that the fleet might be detained at Spithead till she could receive a letter from her friends; but in this she was disappointed, for the second morning after she went on board the signal was made, the fleet8 weighed anchor, and in a few hours (the wind being favorable) they bade adieu to the white cliffs of Albion.

In the meantime every enquiry that could be thought of was made by Mr. and Mrs. Temple; for many days did they indulge the fond hope that she was merely gone off to be married, and that when the indissoluble knot was once tied, she would return with the partner she had chosen and entreat their blessing and forgiveness.

===Chinese===

可是蒙特维尔太清楚这封信一旦落到坦普尔先生手里必然引起的后果：于是他明智地决意在甲板上散步时把信撕成碎片，交给尼普顿（Neptune）照管——这位海神方便的话，也许肯、也许不肯替他把碎片捎上岸去。

此时夏洛特的一切希望和心愿都聚成了一条：但愿舰队能在斯皮特黑德（Spithead）滞留到她收到亲友的来信；可这一点她也落了空，因为她上船后的第二个早晨，信号升起了，舰队[8]起锚了，不出几个钟头（风势顺利），他们便向阿尔比恩（Albion）的白色峭壁道了别。

与此同时，坦普尔先生和坦普尔夫人用尽了一切想得到的打探之方；许多天里，他们纵着那痴心的指望，想她不过是去成了亲，等那解不开的结一系上，她就会带着自己选中的伴侣回来，恳求他们的祝福和宽恕。

===Original===

“And shall we not forgive her?” said Mr. Temple.

“Forgive her!” exclaimed the mother. “Oh! yes; whatever be her errors, is she not our child? And though bowed to the earth even with shame and remorse, is it not our duty to raise the poor penitent and whisper peace and comfort to her desponding soul? would she but return, with rapture would I fold her to my heart and bury every remembrance of her faults in the dear embrace.”

But still, day after day passed on and Charlotte did not appear, nor were any tidings to be heard of her: yet each rising morning was welcomed by some new hope﻿—the evening brought with it disappointment. At length hope was no more; despair usurped her place, and the mansion which was once the mansion of peace became the habitation of pale, dejected melancholy.

===Chinese===

“我们还不肯饶她么？”坦普尔先生说。

“饶她！”做母亲的喊道。“哦！肯的；无论她有什么错处，她难道不是我们的孩子么？哪怕她被羞愧和悔恨压弯到尘埃里，把这可怜的忏悔者扶起来、对她绝望的灵魂低声说些安宁宽慰的话，不正是我们的本分么？只要她肯回来，我会狂喜地把她搂进怀里，把对她过错的每一分记忆，都埋进那亲爱的拥抱之中。”

可是一天又一天过去，夏洛特没有露面，也听不到她的一点音讯：然而每一个新升的早晨都带着新的希望受到欢迎——傍晚随之而来的是失望。终于，希望不复存在；绝望僭据了它的位置；那座曾是安宁之宅的宅邸，变成了苍白沮丧的忧郁之所。

===Original===

The cheerful smile that was wont to adorn the face of Mrs. Temple was fled, and had it not been for the support of unaffected piety, and a consciousness of having ever set before her child the fairest example, she must have sunk under this heavy affliction.

“Since,” said she, “the severest scrutiny can not charge me with any breach of duty, to have deserved this severe chastisement, I will bow before the Power who inflicts it with humble resignation to His will; nor shall the duty of a wife be totally absorbed in the feelings of the mother; I will endeavor to appear more cheerful, and by appearing in some measure to have conquered my own sorrow, alleviate the sufferings of my husband, and rouse him from that torpor into which this misfortune has plunged him. My father, too, demands my care and attention: I must not, by a selfish indulgence of my own grief, forget the interest those two dear objects take in my happiness or misery: I will wear a smile on my face, though the thorn rankles in my heart; and if by so doing, I in the smallest degree contribute to restore their peace of mind, I shall be amply rewarded for the pain the concealment of my own feelings may occasion.”

Thus argued this excellent woman: and in the execution of so laudable a resolution, we shall leave her to follow the fortunes of the hapless victim of imprudence and evil counselors.


===Chinese===

往日点缀着坦普尔夫人脸颊的那副欢欣的笑容不见了；若不是有质朴的虔敬支撑着，又自觉曾在女儿面前立过最端正的榜样，她早就被这沉重的打击压垮了。

“既然，”她说，“最严苛的细察也找不出我有半分亏职、该受这般严厉的惩戒，那么我愿怀着恭顺的听命，向降下这惩戒的大能俯首；为妻的本分也不该被为母的情感完全吞没；我要勉力显得欢快些，借表面上多少战胜了自己的悲伤，来减轻我丈夫的痛苦，把他从这桩不幸使他陷入的麻木中唤起来。我的父亲也需要我的照料和挂心：我决不能因放纵自己的哀伤，就忘了那两个亲爱的人对我的幸福或忧苦的牵挂：我脸上要挂着笑，哪怕心头的荆棘还在刺痛；倘若这样做能为恢复他们心境的安宁尽到一分力，那么掩饰自己的情感纵然带来痛苦，我也得到了丰厚的报偿。”

这位贤淑的妇人便是这样自持：在她践行这一可嘉决心的同时，我们且撇下她，去追随那个轻率与恶友手中的不幸牺牲者的命运。

## XVI Necessary Digression / XVI 必要的题外话

===Original===

On board of the ship on which Charlotte and mademoiselle were embarked, was an officer of large, unencumbered fortune and elevated rank, and whom I shall call Crayton.

He was one of those men who, having traveled in their youth, pretend to have contracted a peculiar fondness for everything foreign, and to hold in contempt the productions of their own country; and this affected partiality extended even to the women.

With him, therefore, the blushing modesty and unaffected simplicity of Charlotte passed unnoticed; but the forward pertness of La Rue, the freedom of her conversation, the elegance of her person, mixed with a certain engaging je ne sais quoi, perfectly enchanted him.

The reader, no doubt, has already developed the character of La Rue: designing, artful and selfish, she had accepted the devoirs of Belcour because she was heartily weary of the retired life she led at the school, wished to be released from what she deemed a slavery, and to return to that vortex of folly and dissipation, which had once plunged her into the deepest misery; but her plan, she flattered herself, was now better formed: she resolved to put herself under the protection of no man, till she had first secured a settlement; but the clandestine manner in which she left Madame Du Pont’s prevented her putting this plan in execution, though Belcour solemnly protested he would make her a handsome settlement the moment they arrived at Portsmouth. This he afterward contrived to evade by a pretended hurry of business: La Rue, readily conceiving he never meant to fulfil his promise, determined to change her battery, and attack the heart of Colonel Crayton. She soon discovered the partiality he entertained for her nation; and having imposed on him a feigned tale of distress, representing Belcour as a villain who had seduced her from her friends under promise of marriage, and afterward betrayed her, pretending great remorse for the errors she had committed, and declaring whatever her affection might have been, it was now entirely extinguished, and she wished for nothing more than an opportunity to leave a course of life which her soul abhorred; but she had no friends to apply to, they had all renounced her, and guilt and misery would undoubtedly be her future portion through life.

===Chinese===

夏洛特和小姐所搭的那艘船上，有一位家道殷实、位阶崇高的军官，我姑且叫他克雷顿（Crayton）。

他是这样一种人：年轻时游历过各地，便假装对一切外国东西养成了特别的爱好，又假意蔑视本国的物产；这做作的偏爱甚至波及女人。

因此，在他眼里，夏洛特那泛红晕的羞怯和天然的素朴全然无足挂齿；而拉吕的前卫泼辣、她谈话的随便、她的仪态优雅、再加上某种说不出的动人风致，却把他完全迷住了。

读者想必早已看穿拉吕的为人：她工于心计、狡诈而自私；她之所以接受贝尔库尔的殷勤，是因为她实在厌倦了在学校里过的隐居生活，巴不得从她所谓的奴役中解脱出来，回到那曾经把她卷入深深不幸的愚行与挥霍的漩涡里去；但她自忖如今盘算得更周到了：她决意在首先确保一份财产之前，绝不把自己置于任何男人的保护之下；只是她逃离迪蓬夫人学校的方式鬼鬼祟祟，使她来不及把这计划付诸实行，尽管贝尔库尔郑重许诺，一到朴次茅斯便给她一份可观的财产。事后他却借口事务匆忙，百般推诿：拉吕一眼看穿他根本无意兑现诺言，便决定调转炮口，去攻打克雷顿上校的心。她很快便发觉他对她的国度怀有好感；于是编了一套身世凄惨的假话，把贝尔库尔说成一个以婚约为饵把她从亲友身边诱走、随后又将她抛弃的恶棍，自己则装出对所犯过失痛悔不已的样子，宣称无论旧日情分如何，如今已烟消云散，她只求有个机会脱离这条为她的灵魂所深恶的生活道路；可她投告无门，亲友都已与她断绝，罪恶和苦难必是她此生注定的份。

===Original===

Crayton was possessed of many amiable qualities, though the peculiar trait in his character, which we have already mentioned, in a great measure threw a shade over them. He was beloved for his humanity and benevolence by all who knew him, but he was easy and unsuspicious himself, and became a dupe to the artifice of others.

He was, when very young, united to an amiable Parisian lady, and perhaps it was his affection for her that laid the foundation for the partiality he ever retained for the whole nation. He had by her one daughter, who entered into the world but a few hours before her mother left it. This lady was universally beloved and admired, being endowed with all the virtues of her mother, without the weakness of the father: she was married to Major Beauchamp, and was at this time in the same fleet with her father, attending her husband to New York.

Crayton was melted by the affected contrition and distress of La Rue; he would converse with her for hours, read to her, play cards with her, listen to all her complaints, and promise to protect her to the utmost of his power. La Rue easily saw his character; her sole aim was to awaken a passion in his bosom that might turn out to her advantage, and in this aim she was but too successful, for before the voyage was finished, the infatuated colonel gave her from under his hand a promise of marriage on their arrival at New York, under forfeiture of five thousand pounds.

===Chinese===

克雷顿具有许多可嘉的品质，只是他性格中我们已提到的那一特点，在很大程度上给这些品质蒙上了一层阴影。凡认识他的人都爱他的仁厚与宽慈，但他自己随和而轻信，便做了他人奸计的冤大头。

他很年轻的时候，娶过一位温婉的巴黎女子，也许正是对她的一片深情，奠下了他终身偏爱整个法兰西民族的根子。她给他留下一个女儿，那女儿降生人世不过几个钟头，母亲便离开了人世。这位小姐人见人爱，她兼有母亲的一切美德，却没有父亲的弱点：她嫁给了博尚少校（Major Beauchamp），此刻正随父亲同在这支舰队里，陪丈夫前往纽约（New York）。

拉吕装出的悔恨与凄苦把克雷顿融化了；他肯陪她一谈几个钟头，读书给她听，陪她打牌，听她诉不完的苦，并允诺竭尽所能保护她。拉吕一眼便看穿了他的性格；她惟一的目标，就是在他胸中唤起一种可以为她所用的激情；而这个目标她是太成功了——航程还未结束，这位神魂颠倒的上校便亲笔立据，允诺一到纽约便与她成婚，违者罚金五千镑。

===Original===

And how did our poor Charlotte pass her time during a tedious and tempestuous passage? Naturally delicate, the fatigue and sickness which she endured rendered her so weak as to be almost entirely confined to her bed; yet the kindness and attention of Montraville, in some measure contributed to alleviate her sufferings, and the hope of hearing from her friends soon after their arrival, kept up her spirits, and cheered many a gloomy hour.

But during the voyage a great revolution took place, not only in the fortune of La Rue, but in the bosom of Belcour: whilst in the pursuit of his amour with mademoiselle, he had attended little to the interesting, inobtrusive charms of Charlotte; but when, cloyed by possession, and disgusted with the art and dissimulation of one, he beheld the simplicity and gentleness of the other, the contrast became too striking not to fill him at once with surprise and admiration. He frequently conversed with Charlotte; he found her sensible, well informed, but diffident and unassuming. The langor which the fatigue of her body and perturbation of her mind spread over her delicate features, served only, in his opinion, to render her more lovely: he knew that Montraville did not design to marry her, and he formed a resolution to endeavor to gain her himself, whenever Montraville should leave her.

Let not the reader imagine Belcour’s designs were honorable. Alas! when once a woman has forgot the respect due to herself by yielding to the solicitations of illicit love, she loses all her consequence, even in the eyes of the man whose art has betrayed them, and for whose sake they have sacrificed every valuable consideration.

===Chinese===

那么，我们可怜的夏洛特在漫长而风雨颠簸的航程中是怎么熬过来的呢？她天生娇弱，一路的劳顿和晕船把她折磨得虚弱不堪，几乎整日困在床上；不过蒙特维尔的体贴和照应，多少减轻了她的痛苦，而一到岸便可听到亲友音讯的希望，也支撑着她的精神，给许多阴郁的时辰添了一点亮色。

但就在这段航程中，不仅拉吕的际遇发生了大变化，贝尔库尔的胸怀也起了大革命：当他追逐着与小姐的情事时，对夏洛特那些含蓄不张扬的动人风韵并未留意；可一旦对到手的猎物生了腻，厌恶了一个人的心机与做作，再看另一个的单纯与温婉，这对照便鲜明得叫他登时又惊又慕。他常同夏洛特交谈；发觉她明理而有见识，却又腼腆而不自负。身体的疲惫与心绪的烦乱给她娇柔容貌笼上的那层慵倦，在他看来，只让她更加可爱：他知道蒙特维尔并不打算娶她，便打定主意：一旦蒙特维尔把她撇下，便设法自己把她弄到手。

读者切莫以为贝尔库尔存的是什么光明正大的念头。唉！一个女人一旦屈从于苟且爱情的撺掇，忘了对自己的尊重，她便失去了全部的分量——即便在那个用手段诱她堕落的男人眼里，在她为之牺牲了一切可贵考虑的那个男人眼里，也是如此。

===Original===

The heedless Fair, who stoops to guilty joys,
					

					A man may pity﻿—but he must despise.

Nay, every libertine will think he has a right to insult her with his licentious passions; and should the unhappy creature shrink from the insolent overture, he will sneeringly taunt her with pretence of modesty.


===Chinese===

轻率的佳人，自贬而就罪中之欢，

男人或会怜她——却必致轻贱。

不止如此；每一个浪荡子都会觉得，他有权拿他淫邪的情欲去侮辱她；那不幸的人若对这无礼的挑逗畏缩退避，他还要冷笑揶揄，讥她假装正经。

## XVII A Wedding / XVII 一场婚礼

===Original===

On the day before their arrival at New York, after dinner, Crayton arose from his seat, and placing himself by mademoiselle, thus addressed the company﻿—

“As we are now nearly arrived at our destined port, I think it but my duty to inform you, my friends, that this lady,” (taking her hand) “has placed herself under my protection. I have seen and severely felt the anguish of her heart, and through every shade which cruelty or malice may throw over her, can discover the most amiable qualities. I thought it but necessary to mention my esteem for her before our disembarkation, as it is my fixed resolution, the morning after we land, to give her an undoubted title to my favor and protection by honorably uniting my fate to hers. I would wish every gentleman hence, therefore, to remember that her honor henceforth is mine; and,” continued he, looking at Belcour, “should any man presume to speak in the least disrespectfully of her, I shall not hesitate to pronounce him a scoundrel!”

Belcour cast at him a smile of contempt, and bowing profoundly low, wished mademoiselle much joy in the proposed union; and assuring the colonel that he need not be in the least apprehensive of anyone throwing the least odium on the character of his lady, shook him by the hand with ridiculous gravity, and left the cabin.

The truth was, he was glad to be rid of La Rue, and so he was but freed from her, he cared not who fell a victim to her infamous arts.

===Chinese===

抵达纽约前一天，饭后，克雷顿从座位上站起来，挨着小姐坐下，向众人这样致辞——

“我们眼看就要抵达目的地了，我认为自己有责任告诉各位朋友，这位女士，”（他执起她的手）“已将自己置于我的保护之下。我亲眼目睹、也切身体味过她心头的绞痛；无论残忍或恶毒在她身上投下怎样的阴影，我都能从中辨认出最可爱的品性。我想有必要在登陆之前把我对她的敬重讲明，因为我心意已决：上岸后的第二天早晨，便以体面的结缡把我的命运与她的连在一起，使她对我的眷顾和保护取得无可置疑的名分。因此，我愿请各位绅士从此记住：她的名誉从今以后就是我的名誉；还有，”他望着贝尔库尔，继续说，“倘若有人胆敢对她说半句不敬的话，我会毫不迟疑地宣布他是恶棍！”

贝尔库尔朝他投去轻蔑的一笑，深深一躬，祝小姐在这门拟议的婚事中获得莫大欢喜；又向上校保证，丝毫不必担心有谁会往他夫人的名节上泼半点污水；随后带着可笑的庄重同他握了握手，出了船舱。

实情是，他巴不得甩掉拉吕；只要能脱开她，他才不管谁去做她那无耻手腕的牺牲品。

===Original===

The inexperienced Charlotte was astonished at what she heard. She thought La Rue had, like herself, only been urged by the force of her attachment to Belcour, to quit her friends, and follow him to the seat of war: how wonderful, then, that she should resolve to marry another man! It was certainly extremely wrong. It was indelicate. She mentioned her thoughts to Montraville. He laughed at her simplicity, called her a little idiot, and patting her on the cheek, said she knew nothing of the world.

“If the world sanctifies such things, ’tis a very bad world I think,” said Charlotte. “Why, I always understood they were to have been married when they arrived at New York. I am sure mademoiselle told me Belcour promised to marry her.”

“Well, and suppose he did?”

“Why, he should be obliged to keep his word, I think.”

===Chinese===

不谙世事的夏洛特听了这些话，惊愕不已。她原以为拉吕同她自己一样，也只是被对贝尔库尔的情意所驱使，才离开亲友，随他奔赴战地：那么，她竟会决意另嫁别人，这多么不可思议啊！这当然是极端错误的。这是有失体统的。她把这些想法说给蒙特维尔听。他笑她天真，叫她小傻瓜，拍拍她的脸颊，说她全然不懂世事。

“若是这世界竟认可这种事，我看这世界可真够坏的，”夏洛特说。“嗳，我一向听说他们到了纽约就要成婚的。我记得清清楚楚，小姐亲口告诉我，贝尔库尔答应娶她的。”

“是呀，那又怎么样呢？”

“嗐，我认为他就该守约。”

===Original===

“Well, but I suppose he has changed his mind,” said Montraville, “and then, you know, the case is altered.”

Charlotte looked at him attentively for a moment. A full sense of her own situation rushed upon her mind. She burst into tears, and remained silent. Montraville too well understood the cause of her tears. He kissed her cheek, and bidding her not make herself uneasy, unable to bear the silent but keen remonstrance, hastily left her.

The next morning by sunrise they found themselves at anchor before the city of New York. A boat was ordered to convey the ladies on shore. Crayton accompanied them; and they were shown to a house of public entertainment. Scarcely were they seated, when the door opened and the colonel found himself in the arms of his daughter, who had landed a few minutes before him. The first transport of meeting subsided, Crayton introduced his daughter to Mademoiselle La Rue as an old friend of her mother’s, (for the artful Frenchwoman had really made it appear to the credulous colonel that she was in the same convent with his first wife, and though much younger, had received many tokens of her esteem and regard).

“If, mademoiselle,” said Mrs. Beauchamp, “you were the friend of my mother, you must be worthy the esteem of all good hearts.”

===Chinese===

“可我想他已经变了主意，”蒙特维尔说，“那么你知道，情形就不同了。”

夏洛特凝神看了他片刻。对自己处境的全盘了悟涌上她的心头。她泪如泉涌，默然无语。蒙特维尔太明白这眼泪的缘由了。他吻了吻她的脸颊，嘱咐她不要自寻烦恼，却再受不了这无声而尖锐的责备，匆匆离开了她。

次日日出时分，他们发现自己已停泊在纽约城前。一条小艇奉命送女士们上岸。克雷顿随行；他们被引到一家公共客栈。刚一落座，门开了，上校发现自己已被女儿搂在怀里——她比他早几分钟登岸。重逢的头一阵激动平息后，克雷顿把女儿介绍给拉吕小姐，说她是她母亲的一位旧友（因为这工于心计的法国女人，果真让那轻信的上校相信，她同他的原配在同一座修道院里待过，虽说年轻得多，却颇蒙她母亲错爱，得过她不少眷顾的表示）。

“小姐，”博尚夫人（Mrs. Beauchamp）说，“您既是我母亲的朋友，想必值得一切善良的心敬重。”

===Original===

“Mademoiselle will soon honor our family,” said Crayton, “by supplying the place that valuable woman filled: and as you are married, my dear, I think you will not blame﻿—”

“Hush, my dear sir,” replied Mrs. Beauchamp: “I know my duty too well to scrutinize your conduct. Be assured, my dear father, your happiness is mine. I shall rejoice in it, and sincerely love the person who contributes to it. But tell me,” continued she, turning to Charlotte, “who is this lovely girl? Is she your sister, mademoiselle?”

A blush, deep as the glow of the carnation, suffused the cheeks of Charlotte.

“It is a young lady,” replied the colonel, “who came in the same vessel with us from England.” He then drew his daughter aside and told her in a whisper, that Charlotte was the mistress of Montraville.

“What a pity!” said Mrs. Beauchamp, softly, (casting a most compassionate glance at her). “But surely her mind is not depraved. The goodness of her heart is depicted in her ingenuous countenance.”

===Chinese===

“小姐很快就要光临我们家了，”克雷顿说，“补上那位可贵的女子留下的位置：亲爱的，你既已出嫁，我想你不会见怪——”

“嘘，亲爱的爸爸，”博尚夫人答道：“我太明白自己的本分，不会来细究您的行事。请放心，亲爱的父亲，您的幸福就是我的幸福。我将为它欢喜，并且真心爱那位成全它的人。可是告诉我，”她转向夏洛特，继续说，“这位可爱的姑娘是谁？她是您的妹妹吗，小姐？”

一阵深如香石竹之艳的红晕涌上夏洛特的双颊。

“是一位年轻小姐，”上校答道，“同我们乘一条船从英格兰来的。”随后他把女儿拉到一旁，悄声告诉她，夏洛特是蒙特维尔的情妇。

“多可惜呀！”博尚夫人轻声说（朝她投去满怀怜惜的一瞥）。“不过，她的心灵一定没有堕落。她心地之善良，正画在她坦率的脸上。”

===Original===

Charlotte caught the word pity. “And am I already fallen so low?” said she. A sigh escaped her, and a tear was ready to start, but Montraville appeared, and she checked the rising emotion. Mademoiselle went with the colonel and his daughter to another apartment. Charlotte remained with Montraville and Belcour. The next morning the colonel performed his promise, and La Rue became in due form Mrs. Crayton, exulted in her good fortune, and dared to look with an eye of contempt on the unfortunate but far less guilty Charlotte.


===Chinese===

夏洛特听到了“怜惜”这个词。“我已经跌得这么低了么？”她说。一声叹息逸出，一滴泪就要夺眶而出，恰好蒙特维尔来了，她按下了涌起的激动。小姐随上校和他的女儿去了另一间屋子。夏洛特同蒙特维尔和贝尔库尔留了下来。第二天早晨，上校履行了诺言，拉吕如愿以偿地做了克雷顿夫人（Mrs. Crayton），为自己的好运扬扬得意，竟敢用轻蔑的眼光看那不幸而罪愆远轻于她的夏洛特。

## XVIII Reflections / XVIII 感怀

===Original===

“And am I indeed fallen so low,” said Charlotte, “as to be only pitied? Will the voice of approbation no more meet my ear? And shall I never again possess a friend whose face will wear a smile of joy whenever I approach? Alas! how thoughtless, how dreadfully imprudent have I been! I know not which is most painful to endure﻿—the sneer of contempt, or the glance of compassion which is depicted in the various countenances of my own sex: they are both equally humiliating. Ah! my dear parents, could you now see the child of your affections, the daughter whom you so dearly loved, a poor, solitary being, without society, here wearing out her heavy hours in deep regret and anguish of heart, no kind friend of her own sex to whom she can unbosom her griefs, no beloved mother, no woman of character to appear in my company; and low as your Charlotte is fallen, she can not associate with infamy.”

These were the painful reflections which occupied the mind of Charlotte. Montraville had placed her in a small house a few miles from New York: he gave her one female attendant, and supplied her with what money she wanted; but business and pleasure so entirely occupied his time, that he had little to devote to the woman whom he had brought from all her connections, and robbed of innocence. Sometimes, indeed, he would steal out at the close of evening, and pass a few hours with her. And then, so much was she attached to him, that all her sorrows were forgotten while blessed with his society: she would enjoy a walk by moonlight, or sit by him in a little arbor at the bottom of the garden, and play on the harp, accompanying it with her plaintive, harmonious voice. But often, very often, did he promise to renew his visits, and forgetful of his promise, leave her to mourn her disappointment. What painful hours of expectation would she pass! she would sit at a window which looked toward a field he used to cross, counting the minutes and straining her eyes to catch the first glimpse of his person, till, blinded with tears of disappointment, she would lean her head on her hands, and give free vent to her sorrows: then catching at some new hope, she would again renew her watchful position till the shades of evening enveloped every object in a dusky cloud: she would then renew her complaints, and, with a heart bursting with disappointed love and wounded sensibility, retire to a bed which remorse had strewed with thorns, and court in vain that comforter of weary nature (who seldom visits the unhappy) to come and steep her senses in oblivion.

Who can form an adequate idea of the sorrow that preyed upon the mind of Charlotte? The wife, whose breast glows with affection to her husband, and who in return meets only indifference, can but faintly conceive her anguish. Dreadfully painful is the situation of such a woman, but she has many comforts of which our poor Charlotte was deprived. The duteous, faithful wife, though treated with indifference, has one solid pleasure within her own bosom; she can reflect that she has not deserved neglect﻿—that she has ever fulfilled the duties of her station with the strictest exactness; she may hope by constant assiduity and unremitted attention to recall her wanderer, and be doubly happy in his returning affection; she knows he can not leave her to unite himself to another: he can not cast her out to poverty and contempt.

===Chinese===

“我当真跌得这么低了么，”夏洛特说，“只配被人怜惜？赞许的声音再也不会进我的耳朵了么？我再也不会有一个朋友，一见我走近便满面笑容了么？唉！我曾多么轻率，多么骇人地不知深浅啊！我不知道哪一样更难捱——是鄙夷的冷笑，还是同情的一瞥，它们画在我同性的一张张脸上：两样同样羞人。啊！我亲爱的父母，你们若能看见你们钟爱的孩子、你们深爱的女儿，如今成了一个可怜的、孤零零的人，无人来往，在这里用深切的悔恨和心头的绞痛捱着沉重的时辰，没有一个同性好友可以倾吐悲伤，没有亲爱的母亲，没有一个有身份的女子肯在众人面前与我为伴；你们的夏洛特纵然跌得这样低，她也决不肯与无耻为伍。”

这些便是盘踞在夏洛特心头的惨痛思虑。蒙特维尔把她安置在离纽约几英里的一座小宅里：给了她一名女仆，供给她一切用度；但事务和行乐占满了他的光阴，使他几乎没有余暇给这个被他拖离一切亲情、夺去了清白的女子。有时，他也确会在暮色四合时溜出来，陪她消磨几个钟头。而她是那样依恋他，只要有他相伴，一切愁苦都被忘却：她喜欢在月光下散步，或是同他坐在花园尽头的凉亭里，弹起竖琴，用哀婉和谐的歌喉伴唱。可是他常常、常常许诺再来，转头便忘了诺言，撇下她为失望哀恸。她在期待中捱过多少难熬的时辰啊！她会坐在一扇朝着他惯常经过的那片田野的窗前，数着分钟，睁大眼睛盼能第一眼望见他的身影，直到失望的泪水糊住了双眼，才把头伏在手上，任悲哀尽情倾泻：随后又抓住一线新的希望，重新守在窗前，直到暮色把万物裹进昏沉的云霭：她于是重新哀叹，带着一颗被失望的爱情和受伤的敏感胀满了的心，退到一张悔恨铺满荆棘的床上，徒然央求那位疲惫天性的安慰者（她很少降临到不幸者身边）前来，把她的知觉浸入遗忘。

谁能充分想见啃噬着夏洛特心头的那份悲愁？一个对丈夫满怀挚爱、回报却只有冷淡的妻子，也只能约略体会她的绞痛。这样一个女子的处境苦痛已极，但她享有许多可怜的夏洛特被剥夺了的安慰。那个尽本分、忠实的妻子，纵然被冷淡相待，胸中也自有一份坚实的快乐；她可以自省并未亏欠这份冷落——她始终一丝不苟地履行了自己名分上的职责；她可以指望以不懈的殷勤和无微不至的体贴，唤回她那游荡的人，在他回头的爱里享受加倍的幸福；她知道他不能离开她去与别人结合：他不能把她一脚踢给贫穷和鄙视。

===Original===

She looks around her and sees the smile of friendly welcome or the tear of affectionate consolation on the face of every person whom she favors with her esteem; and from all these circumstances she gathers comfort: but the poor girl by thoughtless passion led astray, who, in parting with her honor, has forfeited the esteem of the very man to whom she has sacrificed everything dear and valuable in life, feels his indifference in the fruit of her own folly, and laments her want of power to recall his lost affection; she knows there is no tie but honor, and that, in a man who has been guilty of seduction, is but very feeble: he may leave her in a moment to shame and want; he may marry and forsake her forever; and should he, she has no redress, no friendly, soothing companion to pour into her wounded mind the balm of consolation, no benevolent hand to lead her back to the path of rectitude; she has disgraced her friends, forfeited the good opinion of the world, and undone herself; she feels herself a poor solitary being in the midst of surrounding multitudes; shame bows her to the earth, remorse tears her distracted mind, and guilt, poverty, and disease close the dreadful scene: she sinks unnoticed to oblivion. The finger of contempt may point out to some passing daughter of youthful mirth the humble bed where lies this frail sister of mortality; and will she, in the unbounded gayety of her heart, exult in her own unblemished fame and triumph over the silent ashes of the dead? Oh, no! has she a heart of sensibility; she will stop and thus address the unhappy victim of folly﻿—

“Thou hadst thy faults, but sure thy sufferings have expiated them: thy errors brought thee to an early grave; but thou wert a fellow creature﻿—thou hast been unhappy﻿—then be those errors forgotten.”

Then, as she stoops to pluck the noxious weed from off the sod, a tear will fall and consecrate the spot to Charity.

===Chinese===

她环顾四周，凡她青眼相加的人，脸上都有友善的微笑或深情安慰的泪光；从这一切里她汲取安慰：可那被轻率的激情引入歧途的可怜姑娘，在交出名誉的同时，也失去了那个她为之牺牲了人生一切珍重之物的男人的敬重，在他的冷淡里尝到自己愚行的果子，哀叹自己无力唤回他失去的爱；她知道除了名誉再没有别的维系，而在一个犯过诱骗之罪的男人身上，这维系是何等薄弱：他可以转眼把她丢给羞辱和匮乏；他可以娶妻，从此把她抛弃；一旦如此，她没有补救，没有友善温存的伴侣把安慰的香膏倾进她受伤的心，没有仁慈的手引她回到正路；她已使亲友蒙羞，失去了世人的好评，毁掉了自己；她感到自己是四周人海中一个可怜的孤魂；羞耻把她压向尘土，悔恨撕裂她纷乱的心，而罪愆、贫穷和疾病给这可怕的一幕收场：她无声无息地沉入遗忘。轻蔑的手指或许会指给某个路过的青春欢笑的姑娘看，那方埋着这脆弱的凡间姐妹的寒微之床；难道她要趁着心头无边的欢快，夸耀自己无瑕的名声，在死者的静默灰烬上得胜么？哦，不！只要她有一颗善感的心，她便会驻足，对这愚行的不幸牺牲者这样说——

“你有过你的过失，但你受的苦必定已经抵赎了它们：你的过错把你早早送进了坟墓；但你本是我们的同类——你不幸过——那么让那些过错都被忘掉吧。”

随后她俯身从草皮上拔去那害草，一滴泪落下，把这块地方祝圣给了仁慈。

===Original===

Forever honored be the sacred drop of humanity; the angel of mercy shall record its source, and the soul from whence it sprang shall be immortal.

My dear madam, contract not your brow into a frown of disapprobation. I mean not to extenuate the faults of those unhappy women who fall victims to guilt and folly; but surely, when we reflect how many errors we are ourselves subject to, how many secret faults lie hid in the recesses of our hearts, which we should blush to have brought into open day (and yet those faults require the lenity and pity of a benevolent judge, or awful would be our prospect of futurity). I say, my dear madam, when we consider this, we surely may pity the faults of others.

Believe me, many an unfortunate female, who has once strayed into the thorny paths of vice, would gladly return to virtue were any generous friend to endeavor to raise and reassure her; but alas! it can not be, you say; the world would deride and scoff. Then let me tell you, madam, it is a very unfeeling world, and does not deserve half the blessings which a bountiful Providence showers upon it.

Oh, thou benevolent Giver of all good! how shall we erring mortals dare to look up to thy mercy in the great day of retribution, if we now uncharitably refuse to overlook the errors, or alleviate the miseries of our fellow creatures!


===Chinese===

愿这神圣的人性之泪永远受尊崇；仁慈的天使会记下它的来处，它涌出的那个灵魂将得不朽。

我亲爱的夫人，请别把眉头蹙成不以为然的皱痕。我无意替那些做了罪恶与愚行的牺牲品的不幸女子开脱罪责；可是，当我们想到自己易犯多少过错，想到多少隐秘的过失藏在我们心的深处、一旦曝光便会叫我们脸红（而这些过失也正需要一位仁厚审判者的宽宥与怜悯，否则我们前程何其可畏）——我说，我亲爱的夫人，想到这些，我们总该可以怜惜别人的过失了吧。

相信我，许多误入罪恶荆棘路的不幸女子，只要有慷慨的朋友肯出力扶她一把、给她宽慰，都会乐于重回美德；可是唉！不行，您会说；世人会讥笑嘲弄。那么让我告诉您，夫人，这世道实在冷酷，实在配不上慷慨的上天洒给它的那一半福分。

哦，您这一切善的仁厚赐予者！到了大清算的日子，倘若我们现在不肯宽宥同胞的过错、不肯减轻他们的苦难，我们这些易错的凡人，又怎敢仰望您的慈悲！

## XIX A Mistake Discovered / XIX 察觉一个错误

===Original===

Julia Franklin9 was the only child of a man of large property, who at the age of eighteen left her independent mistress of an unencumbered income of seven hundred a year; she was a girl of a lively disposition, and humane, susceptible heart; she resided in New York with an uncle who loved her too well, and had too high an opinion of her prudence, to scrutinize her actions so much as would have been necessary with many young ladies who were not blessed with her discretion: she was, at the time Montraville arrived at New York, the life of society, and the universal toast. Montraville was introduced to her by the following accident.

One night when he was upon guard, a dreadful fire broke out near Mr. Franklin’s house, which in a few hours reduced that and several others to ashes; fortunately no lives were lost, and by the assiduity of the soldiers much valuable property was saved from the flames. In the midst of the confusion an old gentleman came up to Montraville, and putting a small box into his hands, cried﻿—“Keep it, my good sir, till I come to you again;” and then rushing again into the thickest of the crowd, Montraville saw him no more. He waited till the fire was quite extinguished, and the mob dispersed, but in vain: the old gentleman did not appear to claim his property; and Montraville, fearing to make any enquiry, lest he should meet with impostors who might lay claim without any legal right to the box, carried it to his lodgings, and locked it up: he naturally imagined that the person who committed it to his care knew him, and would in a day or two reclaim it; but several weeks passed on, and no enquiry being made, he began to be uneasy, and resolved to examine the contents of the box, and if they were, as he supposed, valuable, to spare no pains to discover and restore them to the owner. Upon opening it, he found it contained jewels to a large amount, about two hundred pounds in money, and a miniature picture set for a bracelet. On examining the picture, he thought he had somewhere seen features very like it, but could not recollect where. A few days after, being at a public assembly, he saw Miss Franklin, and the likeness was too evident to be mistaken: he inquired among his brother officers if any of them knew her, and found one who was upon terms of intimacy in the family: “then introduce me to her immediately,” said he, “for I am certain I can inform her of something which will give her peculiar pleasure.”

He was immediately introduced, found she was the owner of the jewels, and was invited to breakfast the next morning, in order to their restoration. This whole evening Montraville was honored with Julia’s hand; the lively sallies of her wit, the elegance of her manner, powerfully charmed him: he forgot Charlotte, and indulged himself in saying everything that was polite and tender to Julia. But on retiring, recollection returned. “What am I about?” said he: “though I can not marry Charlotte, I can not be villain enough to forsake her, nor must I dare to trifle with the heart of Julia Franklin. I will return this box,” said he, “which has been the source of so much uneasiness already, and in the evening pay a visit to my poor, melancholy Charlotte, and endeavor to forget this fascinating Julia.”

He arose, dressed himself, and taking the picture out, “I will reserve this from the rest,” said he, “and by presenting it to her when she thinks it is lost, enhance the value of the obligation.” He repaired to Mr. Franklin’s, and found Julia in the breakfast parlor alone.

===Chinese===

朱莉娅·富兰克林（Julia Franklin）[9]是一位家资豪富者的独女；十八岁上，父亲故去，她成了一份毫无拖累、岁入七百镑的家产的独立女主人；她天性活泼，心地仁厚多情；她住在纽约，同一位叔父一起，那叔父太爱她，也太信她的审慎，不像对待许多没有她那般分寸的小姐那样事事细究：蒙特维尔抵达纽约时，她正是社交界的灵魂，人人举杯称颂的名媛。蒙特维尔是因下面这桩意外同她相识的。

一天夜里他值岗时，富兰克林先生家附近起了一场可怕的大火，不出几个钟头，便把那座宅子和左近几座都烧成了灰烬；所幸无人丧命，又多亏士兵们奋力抢救，许多值钱的财物从火中保了下来。混乱之中，一位老先生走到蒙特维尔跟前，把一只小盒子塞进他手里，喊道——“替我收着，好先生，等我回来取；”说完又扎进人丛最密处，蒙特维尔再没见到他。他一直等到大火完全扑灭、人群散尽，却是白等：老先生没有来认领他的财物；蒙特维尔又不敢声张打听，惟恐遇上骗子、无缘无故地来认领盒子，便把它带回寓所锁了起来：他自然以为，那把它托付给他的人认得他，一两天内就会来取；可几个星期过去了，无人问津，他开始不安起来，决意打开盒子查看内物，倘若如他所料是贵重之物，便不辞辛苦寻访物主，物归原主。打开一看，里面是价值不菲的珠宝，约两百镑现款，还有一幅镶作手镯饰物的小画像。细看那画像，他觉得在哪儿见过极相似的面容，却想不起在哪里。几天之后，在一次公共聚会上，他见到了富兰克林小姐，那相似之处一望便知，错不了：他向军官同僚们打听有谁认识她，果然有一位同她家过从甚密：“那就立刻引见我，”他说，“因为我确信，我能告诉她一件让她格外欢喜的事。”

他当即被引见，得知她正是珠宝的主人，并被邀次日清晨共进早餐，以便交还原物。这一晚，蒙特维尔荣蒙朱莉娅挽手为伴；她妙语连珠的机锋，她优雅的风度，把他迷住了：他忘了夏洛特，纵情对朱莉娅说尽了殷勤温存的话。可一到退席回寓，记忆回来了。“我在做什么？”他说：“虽说我娶不了夏洛特，我却还不至于卑鄙到抛弃她，也不敢去玩弄朱莉娅·富兰克林的心。我要归还这只盒子，”他说，“它已经惹了这么多不安；晚上再去看望我那可怜的、愁苦的夏洛特，努力忘掉这迷人的朱莉娅。”

他起身穿好衣裳，取出那幅画像，“这一件我要单独留下，”他说，“等她以为它丢了的时候再送还，好教这份人情更加贵重。”他到富兰克林先生家去，发现朱莉娅独自在早餐室里。

===Original===

“How happy am I, madam,” said he, “that being the fortunate instrument of saving these jewels, has been the means of procuring me the acquaintance of so amiable a lady. There are the jewels and money all safe.”

“But where is the picture, sir?” said Julia.

“Here, madam. I would not willingly part with it.”

“It is the portrait of my mother,” said she, taking it from him: “ ’tis all that remains.” She pressed it to her lips, and a tear trembled in her eyes. Montraville glanced his eye on her gray nightgown and black ribbon, and his own feelings prevented a reply.

===Chinese===

“夫人，我多么幸福，”他说，“侥幸做了搭救这些珠宝的工具，还因此得以结识这样一位可爱的女士。珠宝和钱款都在这里，分毫未少。”

“可是那幅画像呢，先生？”朱莉娅说。

“在这里，夫人。我真有点舍不得交出它。”

“这是我母亲的肖像，”她说着，从他手里接过：“她只留下这一点遗物了。”她把它按在唇上，一滴泪在眼里颤动。蒙特维尔瞥了一眼她灰色晨衣和黑缎带，自己的心绪也使他答不出话来。

===Original===

Julia Franklin was the very reverse of Charlotte Temple: she was tall, elegantly shaped, and possessed much of the air and manner of a woman of fashion; her complexion was a clear brown, enlivened with the glow of health; her eyes, full, black, and sparkling, darted their intelligent glances through long silken lashes; her hair was shining brown, and her features regular and striking; there was an air of innocent gayety that played about her countenance where good humor sat triumphant.

“I have been mistaken,” said Montraville. “I imagined I loved Charlotte: but, alas! I am now too late convinced my attachment to her was merely the impulse of the moment. I fear I have not only entailed lasting misery on that poor girl, but also thrown a barrier in the way of my own happiness which it will be impossible to surmount. I feel I love Julia Franklin with ardor and sincerity; yet, when in her presence, I am sensible of my own inability to offer a heart worthy her acceptance, and remain silent.”

Full of these painful thoughts, Montraville walked out to see Charlotte: she saw him approach, and ran out to meet him: she banished from her countenance the air of discontent, which ever appeared when he was absent, and met him with a smile of joy.

“I thought you had forgotten me, Montraville,” said she, “and was very unhappy.”

“I shall never forget you, Charlotte,” he replied, pressing her hand.

===Chinese===

朱莉娅·富兰克林恰是夏洛特·坦普尔的反面：她身材高挑，体态优雅，大有时髦女子的气派和风度；她的肤色是明净的浅褐，透着健康的红润；她的眼睛饱满、乌黑、明亮，聪慧的目光从长长的丝睫间闪射出来；她的头发是亮褐色，五官端正而醒目；她的脸上漾着一种天真的快活，好脾性在那里高奏凯歌。

“我看错了，”蒙特维尔想，“我以为我爱夏洛特：可是唉！如今我才痛悟，我对她的依恋不过是一时的冲动。我怕我不仅把终身的苦难加给了那可怜的姑娘，也在自己幸福的路上投下了一道无法逾越的屏障。我觉得我爱朱莉娅·富兰克林，爱得热烈而真挚；然而一到她面前，我又明白自己拿不出一颗配得上她接受的心，只有沉默。”

蒙特维尔满怀这些苦痛的思虑，出门去看夏洛特：她望见他走近，跑出来迎他：她收起他不在时总浮在脸上的愠色，带着欢喜的笑容迎上前去。

“我还以为你把我忘了呢，蒙特维尔，”她说，“心里正难受。”

“我永远不会忘了你，夏洛特。”他握着她的手答道。

===Original===

The uncommon gravity of his countenance and the brevity of his reply alarmed her.

“You are not well,” said she; “your hand is hot; your eyes are heavy; you are very ill.”

“I am a villain,” said he mentally, as he turned from her to hide his emotions.

“But come,” continued she, tenderly, “you shall go to bed, and I will sit by and watch you; you will be better when you have slept.”

Montraville was glad to retire, and by pretending to sleep, hide the agitation of his mind from her penetrating eye. Charlotte watched by him till a late hour, and then, lying softly down by his side, sunk into a profound sleep, from which she awoke not till late the next morning.


===Chinese===

他脸上异样的沉重和答话的简短使她惊慌起来。

“你不舒服，”她说，“你的手发烫；你的眼睛发沉；你病得不轻。”

“我是个恶棍。”他心里说，一面转过脸去，掩住自己的激动。

“可是来，”她温柔地接着说，“你去躺下，我坐在旁边看着你；睡一觉就会好的。”

蒙特维尔巴不得退下，便装出睡着的样子，把她那双慧眼的注视挡开，掩住心头的激动。夏洛特守着他守到夜深，然后轻轻在他身边躺下，沉入了酣睡，直到第二天很晚的早晨才醒来。

## Virtue﻿—When Most Amiable / 美德——何时不失其可爱

===Original===

“Virtue never appears so amiable as when reaching forth her hand to raise a fallen sister.”

> *Chapter of Accidents*

When Charlotte awoke she missed Montraville; but thinking he might have arisen early to enjoy the beauties of the morning, she was preparing to follow him, when casting her eye on the table, she saw a note, and opening it hastily, found these words﻿—

“My dear Charlotte must not be surprised if she does not see me again for some time: unavoidable business will prevent me that pleasure: be assured I am quite well this morning; and what your fond imagination magnified into illness, was nothing more than fatigue, which a few hours’ rest has entirely removed. Make yourself happy, and be certain of the unalterable friendship of

Montraville.”

“*Friendship!*” said Charlotte, emphatically, as she finished the note. “Is it come to this at last? Alas! poor forsaken Charlotte! Thy doom is now but too apparent. Montraville is no longer interested in thy happiness; and shame, remorse, and disappointed love will henceforth be thy only attendants.”

===Chinese===

“美德从不曾显得这样可爱：当她伸手扶起一个跌倒的姐妹。”

> *《意外之章》（Chapter of Accidents）*

夏洛特醒来时，不见了蒙特维尔；她想他或许起得早，去领略清晨的美景了，正要跟去，目光落在桌上，看见一封短简，急忙拆开，见是这样几行——

“亲爱的夏洛特，倘若有一段时日见不着我，不必惊讶：避不开的事务会夺去我这番乐趣：请放心，我今晨安好；你那多情的想象夸大成疾病的，不过是劳顿罢了，几个钟头的休息已把它完全驱散。你要快活些，并请确信我不可移易的友谊，来自

蒙特维尔。”

“友谊！”夏洛特读完短简，一字一顿地说。“终究到了这一步了么？唉！可怜的被弃的夏洛特！你的劫数如今再明白不过了。蒙特维尔已不再把你的幸福放在心上；羞耻、悔恨和失望的爱情，从此便是你仅有的随侍。”

===Original===

Though these were the ideas that involuntarily rushed upon the mind of Charlotte, as she perused the fatal note, yet, after a few hours had elapsed, the siren Hope again took possession of her bosom, and she flattered herself she could on a second perusal discover an air of tenderness in the few lines he had left, which at first had escaped her notice.

“He certainly can not be so base as to leave me,” said she; “and in styling himself my friend, does he not promise to protect me. I will not torment myself with these causeless fears; I will place a confidence in his honor; and sure he will not be so unjust as to abuse it.”

Just as she had, by this manner of reasoning, brought her mind to some tolerable degree of composure, she was surprised by a visit from Belcour. The dejection visible in Charlotte’s countenance, her swollen eyes and neglected attire, at once told him she was unhappy: He made no doubt but Montraville had, by his coldness, alarmed her suspicions, and was resolved, if possible, to rouse her to jealousy, urge her to reproach him, and by that means occasion a breach between them. “If I can once convince her that she has a rival,” said he, “she will listen to my passion, if it is only to revenge his slights.” Belcour knew but little of the female heart; and what he did know was only of those of loose and dissolute lives. He had no idea that a woman might fall a victim to imprudence, and yet retain so strong a sense of honor as to reject, with horror and contempt, every solicitation to a second fault. He never imagined that a gentle, generous female heart, once tenderly attached, when treated with unkindness, might break, but would never harbor a thought of revenge.

His visit was not long, but before he went, he fixed a scorpion in the heart of Charlotte, whose venom embittered every future hour of her life.

===Chinese===

尽管这些念头是那样不由自主地涌上心头（当她读着那封致命的短简时），几个钟头过后，海妖般的希望重又占据了她的胸怀，她自欺说，再读一遍他留下的那几行字，便能看出一层起初不曾留意的温柔意味。

“他总不至于那样卑鄙，会丢下我，”她说；“他既自称是我的朋友，岂不就答应了要保护我。我不愿拿这些无端的恐惧折磨自己；我要信任他的名誉；他想必不会那样不义，辜负了它。”

她正用这番道理把心境调理得勉强安稳，贝尔库尔的来访叫她吃了一惊。夏洛特脸上可见的憔悴、她哭肿的眼睛和无心打理的衣装，立刻告诉他她不快活：他毫不怀疑，必是蒙特维尔的冷淡惊动了她的疑心，于是打定主意，要就可能挑起她的妒意，怂恿她去责难他，借此促成两人的决裂。“只要我能让她相信她有一个情敌，”他想，“她就会听我倾诉激情，哪怕只是为了报复他的轻慢。”贝尔库尔对女人的心知之甚少；他所知道的那一点，也只是那些放浪度日的女人的心。他想不到一个女子可能做了轻率的牺牲品，却仍保持着那样强烈的名节之感，会带着憎恶和鄙夷拒绝一切再犯一个过错的要求。他从不曾想到，一颗温柔慷慨的女儿心，一旦深情相许，受了薄待，可能会碎，却决不会存报复的念头。

他坐得不久，可临走之前，他在夏洛特心里安放了一只蝎子，它的毒液苦了她此后生命的每一个钟头。

===Original===

We will now return, for a moment, to Colonel Crayton. He had been three months married, and in that little time had discovered that the conduct of his lady was not so prudent as it ought to have been: but remonstrance was vain; her temper was violent; and to the colonel’s great misfortune, he had conceived a sincere affection for her: she saw her own power, and with the art of a Circe, made every action appear to him in what light she pleased: his acquaintances laughed at his blindness, his friends pitied his infatuation, his amiable daughter, Mrs. Beauchamp, in secret deplored the loss of her father’s affection, and grieved that he should be so entirely swayed by an artful and, she much feared, infamous woman.

Mrs. Beauchamp was mild and engaging; she loved not the hurry and bustle of a city, and had prevailed on her husband to take a house a few miles from New York. Chance led her into the same neighborhood with Charlotte; their houses stood within a short space of each other, and their gardens joined. She had not been long in her new habitation before the figure of Charlotte struck her; she recollected her interesting features; she saw the melancholy so conspicuous in her countenance, and her heart bled at the reflection that, perhaps, deprived of honor, friends, all that was valuable in life, she was doomed to linger out a wretched existence in a strange land, and sink brokenhearted into an untimely grave. “Would to Heaven I could snatch her from so hard a fate!” said she; “but the merciless world has barred the doors of compassion against a poor, weak girl, who, perhaps, had she one kind friend to raise and reassure her, would gladly return to peace and virtue; nay, even the woman who dares to pity and endeavor to recall a wandering sister, incurs the sneer of contempt and ridicule, for an action in which even angels are said to rejoice.”

The longer Mrs. Beauchamp was a witness to the solitary life Charlotte led, the more she wished to speak to her; and often as she saw her cheeks wet with the tears of anguish, she would say﻿—“Dear sufferer, how gladly would I pour into your heart the balm of consolation, were it not for the fear of derision.”

But an accident soon happened which made her resolve to brave even the scoffs of the world, rather than not enjoy the heavenly satisfaction of comforting a desponding fellow creature.

===Chinese===

现在让我们回过头来，说一说克雷顿上校。他结婚三个月了，在这短短的时间里，已经发现夫人的行为不像它应该的那样检点：但规劝是徒然的；她的性子暴烈；而使上校大为不幸的是，他对她生出了真情：她看清了自己的权柄，便以喀耳刻（Circe）的手腕，把每一件事在他眼里变成她愿意的样子：相识们笑他的盲目，朋友们怜他的痴迷，他那可爱的女儿博尚夫人，则暗暗哀悼父亲之爱的丧失，痛心他竟被一个工于心计、而且她深恐是无耻的女人完全左右。

博尚夫人温婉可亲；她不喜欢城市的忙乱喧嚣，说服丈夫在离纽约几英里的地方赁了一座宅子。机缘把她引到了夏洛特所在的邻里；两家的宅子相距不远，园子更是相连。她迁入新居不久，夏洛特的身影便引起了她的注意；她记起了她那动人的容貌；她看见她脸上那掩饰不住的忧郁，一想到这女子也许被夺去了名誉、亲友和人生一切可贵之物，注定在异乡捱尽凄凉的一生、心碎而死、埋进早夭的坟墓，她的心便流血。“但愿上天能把她从这样残酷的命运里救出来！”她说；“可这无情的世人，早已把怜悯之门对一个可怜柔弱的姑娘关死了；她呀，只要有一位好友肯扶她一把、给她安慰，多半会乐于重回安宁与美德；不，就连那敢于怜惜、并设法唤回一个迷途姐妹的女子，也要担受讥诮和嘲笑——为了连天使都据说会为之欢欣的善行。”

博尚夫人目睹夏洛特过的孤寂生活越久，越想同她说话；她时常看见她双颊被苦痛的泪浸湿，便会想——“亲爱的受难者，若不是怕人耻笑，我多么乐意把安慰的香膏倾进你的心田。”

但不久一桩意外发生了，使她决意哪怕担受世人的讥嘲，也要享受安慰一个绝望同胞的天国之乐。

===Original===

Mrs. Beauchamp was an early riser. She was one morning walking in the garden, leaning on her husband’s arm, when the sound of a harp attracted their notice: they listened attentively, and heard a soft, melodious voice distinctly sing the following stanzas:10

“Thou glorious orb, supremely bright,
					

					Just rising from the sea,
					

					To cheer all nature with thy light,
					

					What are thy beams to me?

===Chinese===

博尚夫人起得早。一天早晨，她挽着丈夫的手臂在园中散步，一阵竖琴声引起了他们的注意：他们凝神细听，听见一个柔和悦耳的声音，清晰地唱出下面几节歌[10]：

“你这光辉的星体，至为明亮，

正从海上升起，

要用你的光照亮万物，

可你的光芒于我何益？

===Original===

“In vain thy glories bid me rise,
					

					To hail the newborn day,
					

					Alas! my morning sacrifice,
					

					Is still to weep and pray.

“For what are nature’s charms combin’d
					

					To one whose weary breast
					

===Chinese===

“你的荣光唤我起身，

去迎接新生的白昼，皆是徒然；

唉！我清晨的祭献，

依旧只是哭泣与祈祷。

“天地万般景色加在一起，

于一个疲惫的胸怀又有何用——

===Original===

					Can neither peace nor comfort find,
					

					Nor friend whereon to rest?

“Oh! never, never! whilst I live
					

					Can my heart’s anguish cease;
					

					Come, friendly death, thy mandate give,
					

					And let me be at peace.”

===Chinese===

它既寻不见安宁与慰藉，

也没有可以依托的朋友？

“哦！绝不，绝不！只要我还活着，

我心头的悲恸怎能止息；

来吧，友好的死亡，颁下你的敕令，

让我得以安息。”

===Original===

“ ’Tis poor Charlotte!” said Mrs. Beauchamp, the pellucid drop of humanity stealing down her cheek.

Captain Beauchamp was alarmed at her emotion. “What, Charlotte?” said he. “Do you know her?”

In the accent of a pitying angel did she disclose to her husband Charlotte’s unhappy situation, and the frequent wish she had formed of being serviceable to her. “I fear,” continued she, “the poor girl has been basely betrayed; and if I thought you would not blame me, I would pay her a visit, offer her my friendship, and endeavor to restore to her heart that peace she seems to have lost, and so pathetically laments. Who knows, my dear,” laying her hand affectionately on his arm, “who knows but she has left some kind, affectionate parents to lament her errors, and would she return, they might with rapture receive the poor penitent, and wash away her faults in tears of joy? Oh! what a glorious reflection would it be for me could I be the happy instrument of restoring her. Her heart may not be depraved, Beauchamp.”
